\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}}\,\frac{(33/5)x^{2}+(64347/3500)}{x^{4}+(79321/19600)x^{2}+(71289/30625)};

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}}\,\frac{7x^{3}+(59989/2800)x}{x^{4}+(79321/19600)x^{2}+(71289/30625)},

(76)

where
\eta_{1}^{a(4)}(x)=\eta_{2}^{a(4)}(2x)
,
\eta_{3}^{a(4)}(x)=\eta_{4}^{a(4)}(2x)
holds, or with numerical values

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 7.898\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}};

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}}\,\frac{6.600x^{2}+18.385}{x^{4}+4.047x^{2}+2.328};

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}}\,\frac{7x^{3}+21.425x}{x^{4}+4.047x^{2}+2.328}.

(77)

III.7
Electron viscosity
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}
of Braginskii

For a one ion-electron plasma with similar temperatures, the mass-ratio coefficients (
29
), (
30
) simplify into

\displaystyle\hat{K}_{ei(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3\frac{m_{i}}{m_{e}};\qquad\hat{K}_{ei(2)}=\frac{4}{5};\qquad L_{ei(1)}=\frac{9}{35}\frac{m_{i}}{m_{e}};\qquad L_{ei(2)}=\frac{12}{35}\frac{m_{e}}{m_{i}};

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{ei(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{36}{5};\qquad\hat{M}_{ei(2)}=\frac{4}{5}\frac{m_{e}}{m_{i}};\qquad N_{ei(1)}=-\,\frac{12}{35};\qquad N_{ei(2)}=-\,\frac{36}{35}\frac{m_{e}^{2}}{m_{i}^{2}},

(78)

and collisional exchange rates for the viscosity-tensors become

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}_{e}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{21}{10}\nu_{ee}+3\nu_{ei}\Big)\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}+\Big(\frac{9}{70}\nu_{ee}+\frac{9}{35}\nu_{ei}\Big)\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e};

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}_{e}^{(4)}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{53}{20}\nu_{ee}+\frac{36}{5}\nu_{ei}\Big)\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}+\Big(-\frac{79}{140}\nu_{ee}+\frac{12}{35}\nu_{ei}\Big)\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e}.

(79)

Converting everything to
\nu_{ei}
with
\nu_{ee}=\nu_{ei}/(Z_{i}\sqrt{2})
yields

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}_{e}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{21}{10Z_{i}\sqrt{2}}+3\Big)\nu_{ei}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}+\Big(\frac{9}{70Z_{i}\sqrt{2}}+\frac{9}{35}\Big)\nu_{ei}\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e};

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}_{e}^{(4)}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{53}{20Z_{i}\sqrt{2}}+\frac{36}{5}\Big)\nu_{ei}\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}+\Big(-\frac{79}{140Z_{i}\sqrt{2}}+\frac{12}{35}\Big)\nu_{ei}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e},

(80)

and these contributions enter the r.h.s. of evolution equations

\displaystyle\frac{d_{e}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}+\Omega_{e}\big(\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}\big)^{S}+p_{e}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e}=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}_{e}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime};

\displaystyle\frac{d_{e}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e}+\Omega_{e}\big(\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e}\big)^{S}+7\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e}=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(4)}_{e}\,{}^{\prime}.

(81)

In a quasi-static approximation, solution of (
80
), (
81
) yields the electron viscosity tensor
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}
in form
(
70
), (
71
), with electron viscosities

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}\,\frac{5Z_{i}(408Z_{i}+205\sqrt{2})}{6(192Z_{i}^{2}+301Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+178)};

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}\Big[\frac{3\sqrt{2}+6Z_{i}}{5Z_{i}}x^{2}+\frac{3(192Z_{i}^{2}+301Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+178)(408Z_{i}+205\sqrt{2})}{196000Z_{i}^{3}}\Big]/\triangle;

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}x\Big[x^{2}+\frac{119520Z_{i}^{2}+101784\sqrt{2}Z_{i}+46561}{39200Z_{i}^{2}}\Big]/\triangle;

\displaystyle\triangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+\frac{212256Z_{i}^{2}+176376\sqrt{2}Z_{i}+79321}{39200Z_{i}^{2}}x^{2}+\Big(\frac{3(192Z_{i}^{2}+301Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+178)}{700Z^{2}}\Big)^{2},

(82)

where
x=\Omega_{e}/\nu_{ei}
, and relations
\eta_{1}^{e}(x)=\eta_{2}^{e}(2x)
,
\eta_{3}^{e}(x)=\eta_{4}^{e}(2x)
. For the particular case of
Z_{i}=1
these electron viscosities become

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}\,\frac{2040+1025\sqrt{2}}{2220+1806\sqrt{2}};

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}\Big[\frac{3\sqrt{2}+6}{5}x^{2}+\frac{297987}{98000}\sqrt{2}+\frac{82311}{19600}\Big]/\triangle;

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}x\Big[x^{2}+\frac{12723}{4900}\sqrt{2}+\frac{166081}{39200}\Big]/\triangle;

\displaystyle\triangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+\Big(\frac{22047}{4900}\sqrt{2}+\frac{291577}{39200}\Big)x^{2}+\Big(\frac{1431459}{245000}+\frac{14319}{3500}\sqrt{2}\Big),

(83)

or with numerical values

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.73094\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}};

\displaystyle\eta_{2}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}\big(2.049x^{2}+8.500\big)/\triangle;

\displaystyle\eta_{4}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}x\big(x^{2}+7.909\big)/\triangle;

\displaystyle\triangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+13.801x^{2}+11.628,

(84)

recovering the electron viscosity of
Braginskii 1965
, his equation (4.45).
It appears that the Braginskii parallel viscosity value of 0.733 is slightly imprecise and should be 0.731 instead.
The analytic result for parallel viscosity
\eta^{e}_{0}
agrees with
Simakov & Molvig 2014
, and the
value 0.73094 agrees with
Ji & Held 2013
; see the inset of their Figure 3 (curiously,
in a more precise 3-Laguerre approximation the coefficient changes to 0.733). Note that for
x\to 0
viscosity
\eta_{2}^{e}\to\eta_{0}^{e}
.
As discussed previously, our
\Omega_{e}
is negative and in Braginskii it is positive, yielding an opposite sign in front of
\eta_{4}^{e}
.
Braginskii offers electron viscosities only for
Z_{i}=1
.
The analytic result (
82
) is useful to quickly calculate electron viscosities for any
Z_{i}
.
Ji & Held 2013
;
Ji & Held 2015
also
provide useful fitting formulas.

\displaystyle=
