IV
Generalized electron coefficients for multi-species plasmas

Here we use the idea of
Simakov & Molvig 2014
, and before that for example by
Zhdanov 2002
(orig publ. 1982) and
Hinton 1983
,
who pointed out that because of the smallness of mass ratios
m_{e}/m_{i}
,
the electron coefficients of
Braginskii 1965
can be
straightforwardly generalized for multi-species plasmas.
Simakov & Molvig 2014
considered unmagnetized plasmas and provide analytic parallel coefficients

\alpha_{0}
,
\beta_{0}
,
\gamma_{0}
together with the parallel electron viscosity
\eta_{0}^{e}
. Here we show that the same construction
applies when a magnetic field is present, and that all the electron
coefficients provided in the previous section can be easily generalized in the same way.
One starts by considering the general multi-species description with collisional contributions given in Section
II.2
.
Because of the smallness of
m_{e}/m_{i}
, mass-ratio coefficients for each ion species simplify into (
47
).
One introduces an effective ion charge together with an effective ion velocity

Z_{\textrm{eff}}=\frac{\sum_{i}\nu_{ei}}{\sqrt{2}\nu_{ee}}=\frac{\sum_{i}n_{i}Z_{i}^{2}}{n_{e}};\qquad\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\rangle_{\textrm{eff}}=\frac{\sum_{i}\nu_{ei}\boldsymbol{u}_{i}}{\sum_{i}\nu_{ei}},

(88)

and it is straightforward to show that the collisional contributions for a one ion-electron plasma (
48
), (
52
)
are then replaced by

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{R}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\rho_{e}(\sum_{i}\nu_{ei})(\boldsymbol{u}_{e}-\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\rangle_{\textrm{eff}})+\frac{21}{10}\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}(\sum_{i}\nu_{ei})\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}-\frac{3}{56}\frac{\rho_{e}^{2}}{p_{e}^{2}}(\sum_{i}\nu_{ei})\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{e};

(89)

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(3)}_{e}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}p_{e}(\sum_{i}\nu_{ei})(\boldsymbol{u}_{e}-\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\rangle_{\textrm{eff}})-\Big[\frac{\sqrt{2}}{Z_{\textrm{eff}}}+\frac{19}{4}\Big](\sum_{i}\nu_{ei})\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}+\Big[\frac{3}{70\sqrt{2}Z_{\textrm{eff}}}+\frac{69}{560}\Big](\sum_{i}\nu_{ei})\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{e};

(90)

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(5)}_{e}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+27\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}}(\sum_{i}\nu_{ei})(\boldsymbol{u}_{e}-\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\rangle_{\textrm{eff}})-\Big[\frac{76}{5\sqrt{2}Z_{\textrm{eff}}}+\frac{759}{10}\Big](\sum_{i}\nu_{ei})\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}-\Big[\frac{3}{35\sqrt{2}Z_{\textrm{eff}}}-\frac{533}{280}\Big](\sum_{i}\nu_{ei})\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{e}.

(91)

Contributions (
90
), (
91
) enter the right-hand-sides of electron evolution equations (
51
).
The system is completely the same as for the one ion-electron plasma, if in (
48
), (
52
) the following replacement is applied

Z_{i}\to Z_{\textrm{eff}};\qquad\nu_{ei}\to\sum_{i}\nu_{ei};\qquad\delta\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{u}_{e}-\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\to\boldsymbol{u}_{e}-\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\rangle_{\textrm{eff}}.

(92)

If evolution equations can be obtained with the transformation (
92
), of course their solution can be obtained with the same transformation as well.
The same transformation applies for the viscous evolution equations (
80
), (
81
) and their solutions. As an example, the generalized
(thermal) electron heat of
Braginskii 1965
for multi-species plasmas reads

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}^{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{e}-\kappa_{\perp}^{e}\nabla_{\perp}T_{e}+\kappa_{\times}^{e}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{e};

(93)

\displaystyle\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}(\sum_{i}\nu_{ei})}\gamma_{0};\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{e}=\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}(\sum_{i}\nu_{ei})}\frac{\gamma_{1}^{\prime}x^{2}+\gamma_{0}^{\prime}}{\triangle};\qquad\kappa_{\times}^{e}=\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}(\sum_{i}\nu_{ei})}\frac{\gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}x^{3}+\gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime}x}{\triangle};

(94)

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{25Z_{\textrm{eff}}(433Z_{\textrm{eff}}+180\sqrt{2})}{4(217Z_{\textrm{eff}}^{2}+604Z_{\textrm{eff}}\sqrt{2}+288)};\qquad\gamma_{1}^{\prime}=\frac{13Z_{\textrm{eff}}+4\sqrt{2}}{4Z_{\textrm{eff}}};\qquad\gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{5}{2};

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(217Z_{\textrm{eff}}^{2}+604Z_{\textrm{eff}}\sqrt{2}+288)(433Z_{\textrm{eff}}+180\sqrt{2})}{78400Z_{\textrm{eff}}^{3}};\qquad\gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{320797Z_{\textrm{eff}}^{2}+202248Z_{\textrm{eff}}\sqrt{2}+72864}{31360Z_{\textrm{eff}}^{2}};

\displaystyle\triangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{4}+\delta_{1}x^{2}+\delta_{0};\qquad\delta_{0}=\Big(\frac{217Z_{\textrm{eff}}^{2}+604Z_{\textrm{eff}}\sqrt{2}+288}{700Z_{\textrm{eff}}^{2}}\Big)^{2};

\displaystyle\delta_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{586601Z_{\textrm{eff}}^{2}+330152Z_{\textrm{eff}}\sqrt{2}+106016}{78400Z_{\textrm{eff}}^{2}},

(95)

where
x=\Omega_{e}/(\sum_{i}\nu_{ei})
. With recipe (
92
) one obtains generalized solutions for the frictional electron heat flux
\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}^{u}
,
together with solutions for
\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{e}
and viscosity-tensors
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{e}
,
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{e}
which are not repeated here.

From the electron momentum equation, the electric field then becomes

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{E}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{c}\boldsymbol{u}_{e}\times\boldsymbol{B}-\frac{1}{en_{e}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{e}+\frac{m_{e}}{e}\boldsymbol{G}

(96)

\displaystyle+(\sum_{i}\nu_{ei})\Big[+\frac{m_{e}}{e}\big(\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{i}\rangle_{\textrm{eff}}-\boldsymbol{u}_{e}\big)+\frac{21}{10}\frac{m_{e}}{ep_{e}}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}-\frac{3}{56}\frac{\rho_{e}^{2}}{en_{e}p_{e}^{2}}\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{e}\Big]-\frac{m_{e}}{e}\frac{d_{e}\boldsymbol{u}_{e}}{dt},

and expressions for heat fluxes
\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}
and
\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{e}
enter the electric field.

V
Generalization with coupling of stress-tensors and heat fluxes

Here we consider the coupling between viscosity-tensors and heat fluxes.
Using the semi-linear approximation and retaining the coupling,
the 21-moment model (
9
)-(
12
) simplifies into

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\big)^{S}+p_{a}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{a}+\frac{2}{5}\Big((\nabla\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a})^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}_{a}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime}=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(2)}_{a}-\frac{\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}}{3}\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(2)}_{a};

(97)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}+\frac{5}{2}p_{a}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-\frac{5}{2}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}

\displaystyle\qquad=\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(3)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(3)}_{a}-\frac{5}{2}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\boldsymbol{R}_{a};

(98)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}\big)^{S}+7\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{a}+\frac{1}{5}\Big[(\nabla\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a})^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}(\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a})\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}=\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(4)}_{a}-\frac{\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}}{3}\textrm{Tr}\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(4)}_{a};

(99)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+70\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)+18\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-98\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}^{2}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}

\displaystyle\qquad=\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(5)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}=\textrm{Tr}\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(5)}_{a}-35\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}^{2}}\boldsymbol{R}_{a}.

(100)

Terms such as
(\nabla p_{a})\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}
were neglected and large-scale gradients are assumed to be small (see Section
II.4
).
The right-hand-sides were given in Sections
II.1
&
II.2
, and for one ion-electron plasmas
in Section
III
.
The system now represents a generalization of
Braginskii 1965
, where heat fluxes and stress-tensors are coupled.
For the highest-level of precision, one should solve dispersion relations directly with the above system, where all the heat fluxes and stress-tensors are

independent
variables. At the lowest-level of precision, one prescribes the quasi-static approximation and cancels the time-derivatives
d/dt
.
Nevertheless, for sufficiently low frequencies there exists a “middle-route” procedure known from the algebra of collisionless models,
by decomposing each moment into its first and second orders

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}^{(1)}+\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}^{(2)};\qquad\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}=\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{a}+\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,2)}_{a};

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{a}+\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,2)}_{a};\qquad\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{a}+\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,2)}_{a},

(101)

and by neglecting the time derivative of the second-order moments. One can consider

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\big)^{S}+p_{a}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{a}+\frac{2}{5}\Big((\nabla\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}^{(1)})^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}^{(1)}\Big)=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}_{a}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime};

(102)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}\big)^{S}+7\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{a}+\frac{1}{5}\Big[(\nabla\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{a})^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}(\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{a})\Big]=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime};

(103)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}^{(1)}+\Omega_{a}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}+\frac{5}{2}p_{a}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{a}-\frac{5}{2}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{a}=\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(3)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime};

(104)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+70\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)+18\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{a}-98\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}^{2}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{a}=\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(5)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime},

(105)

where the collisional contributions on the right hand sides contain full moments
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}
,
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}
,
\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}
,
\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}
.
In the collisionless regime, a similar procedure was used for example by
Macmahon 1965
,
Mikhailovskii & Smolyakov 1985
,
Ramos 2005
,
Goswami et al. 2005
,

Passot et al. 2012
, and
it is well-known that retaining the time-derivatives
d/dt
is crucial for recovering the dispersion relation of
perpendicular fast mode with respect to kinetic theory (its wavenumber dependence in the long-wavelength limit).
It is straightforward to further increase the precision, by for example retaining full
\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}
&
\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}

in the last terms of (
102
) & (
103
),
or by retaining full
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}
&
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(4)}
in the last terms of (
104
) & (
105
) (which we do not show).
The procedure and its application is described in detail in
Hunana et al. 2019b
(see Sections
5.8 & 5.9), and the coupling of stress-tensors and heat fluxes is also crucial for the firehose instability (see Figures 7 & 10 there; see also
figures with simpler models in
Hunana & Zank 2017
).

VI
Coupling for unmagnetized one ion-electron plasma.

We further focus on the particular case of a one ion-electron plasma with similar temperatures. It is of course possible to algebraically solve
the entire system (
102
)-(
105
) with a magnetic field present, which will be presented elsewhere.
Here for clarity and to demonstrate our point we find it sufficient to focus on an unmagnetized plasma.
Equivalently, we thus only consider solutions for parallel moments along the magnetic field, similarly to the heat flux model of

Spitzer & Härm 1953
.
For the heat flux equations (
102
), (
103
) it is beneficial to introduce matrices

\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Y}}}^{(3,1)}_{a}=(\nabla\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}^{(1)})^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}^{(1)};\qquad\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Y}}}^{(5,1)}_{a}=(\nabla\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{a})^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{a},

(106)

which are symmetric and traceless, analogously to matrix
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{a}
.

VI.1
Ion species (self-collisions)

For the ion species, the viscosity-tensors have the following form

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1025}{1068}\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{a};

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,2)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\nu_{aa}}\Big[\frac{79}{534}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Y}}}^{(3,1)}_{a}+\frac{3}{178}\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Y}}}^{(5,1)}_{a}+\frac{395}{1068}\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{a}}{\partial t}+\frac{15}{178}\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{a}}{\partial t}\Big];

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{8435}{1068}\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}\nu_{aa}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{a};

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,2)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\nu_{aa}}\Big[+\frac{371}{534}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Y}}}^{(3,1)}_{a}-\frac{49}{178}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Y}}}^{(5,1)}_{a}+\frac{1855}{1068}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{a}}{\partial t}-\frac{245}{178}\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{a}}{\partial t}\Big],

(107)

and heat fluxes become

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{125}{32}\frac{p_{a}}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}}\nabla T_{a};

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\nu_{aa}}\Big[+\frac{515}{96}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{a}-\frac{95}{96}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{a}-\frac{5}{48}\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}^{(1)}_{a}}{\partial t}-\frac{5}{96}\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{a}}{\partial t}\Big];

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2975}{24}\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}m_{a}\nu_{aa}}\nabla T_{a};

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,2)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\nu_{aa}}\Big[+\frac{p^{2}_{a}}{\rho^{2}_{a}}\frac{13825}{72}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{a}-\frac{2485}{72}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{a}+\frac{665}{36}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}^{(1)}_{a}}{\partial t}-\frac{175}{72}\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{a}}{\partial t}\Big].

(108)

The model is fully specified and closed, and can be used in the given form. Nevertheless, it is possible to further apply the semi-linear approximation,
in which case the viscosity corrections simplify into

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,2)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{45575}{17088}}_{2.6671}\frac{p_{a}}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}^{2}}\Big[2\nabla\nabla T_{a}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla^{2}T_{a}\Big]+\underbrace{\frac{1164025}{1140624}}_{1.0205}\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}^{2}}\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{a}}{\partial t};

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,2)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{536725}{17088}}_{31.4095}\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}m_{a}\nu_{aa}^{2}}\Big[2\nabla\nabla T_{a}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla^{2}T_{a}\Big]+\underbrace{\frac{10498075}{1140624}}_{9.2038}\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}\nu_{aa}^{2}}\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{a}}{\partial t},

(109)

and the heat flux corrections become

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{45575}{17088}}_{2.6671}\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}\nu_{aa}^{2}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{a}+\underbrace{\frac{31625}{4608}}_{6.8631}\frac{p_{a}}{m_{a}\nu_{aa}^{2}}\frac{\partial\nabla T_{a}}{\partial t};

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,2)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1131725}{12816}}_{88.3056}\frac{p_{a}^{3}}{\rho_{a}^{2}\nu_{aa}^{2}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{a}+\underbrace{\frac{791875}{3456}}_{229.1305}\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}m_{a}\nu_{aa}^{2}}\frac{\partial\nabla T_{a}}{\partial t}.

(110)

VI.2
Electron species

For the electron species, it is useful to introduce denominator

D_{1}=192Z_{i}^{2}+301\sqrt{2}Z_{i}+178,

(111)

and solutions for the stress-tensors are

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{5Z_{i}(205\sqrt{2}+408Z_{i})}{6D_{1}}\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e};

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,2)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{D_{1}\nu_{ei}}\Big[\frac{Z_{i}}{3}(79\sqrt{2}-96Z_{i})\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Y}}}^{(3,1)}_{e}+3Z_{i}(\sqrt{2}+4Z_{i})\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Y}}}^{(5,1)}_{e}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{5}{6}Z_{i}(79\sqrt{2}-96Z_{i})\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{e}}{\partial t}+15Z_{i}(\sqrt{2}+4Z_{i})\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{e}}{\partial t}\Big];

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{35Z_{i}(241\sqrt{2}+552Z_{i})}{6D_{1}}\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}\nu_{ei}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e};

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,2)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{D_{1}\nu_{ei}}\Big[\frac{7}{3}Z_{i}(53\sqrt{2}+288Z_{i})\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Y}}}^{(3,1)}_{e}-7Z_{i}(7\sqrt{2}+20Z_{i})\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Y}}}^{(5,1)}_{e}

(112)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{35}{6}Z_{i}(53\sqrt{2}+288Z_{i})\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{e}}{\partial t}-35Z_{i}(7\sqrt{2}+20Z_{i})\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{e}}{\partial t}\Big],

with matrices
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Y}}}_{e}
defined by (
106
). For the heat fluxes it is useful to define denominator

D_{2}=217Z_{i}^{2}+604Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+288,

(113)

together with
\delta\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{u}_{e}-\boldsymbol{u}_{i}
, and the results read

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{25Z_{i}(180\sqrt{2}+433Z_{i})}{4D_{2}}\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{ei}}\nabla T_{e}+\frac{30Z_{i}(15\sqrt{2}+11Z_{i})}{D_{2}}p_{e}\delta\boldsymbol{u};

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{D_{2}\nu_{ei}}\Big[\frac{5}{4}Z_{i}(1236\sqrt{2}+4097Z_{i})\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{e}-\frac{5}{4}Z_{i}(228\sqrt{2}+709Z_{i})\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{e}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{5}{2}Z_{i}(12\sqrt{2}-533Z_{i})\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}^{(1)}_{e}}{\partial t}-\frac{15}{4}Z_{i}(4\sqrt{2}+23Z_{i})\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{e}}{\partial t}\Big];

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{175Z_{i}(204\sqrt{2}+571Z_{i})}{D_{2}}\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}m_{e}\nu_{ei}}\nabla T_{e}+\frac{840Z_{i}(13\sqrt{2}+12Z_{i})}{D_{2}}\frac{p^{2}_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\delta\boldsymbol{u};

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,2)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{D_{2}\nu_{ei}}\Big[+175Z_{i}(316\sqrt{2}+1103Z_{i})\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}^{2}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{e}-35Z_{i}(284\sqrt{2}+951Z_{i})\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{e}

(114)

\displaystyle\qquad+70Z_{i}(76\sqrt{2}+759Z_{i})\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}^{(1)}_{e}}{\partial t}-175Z_{i}(4\sqrt{2}+19Z_{i})\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{e}}{\partial t}\Big].

The system is now fully specified and can be used in this form. For the particular case of
Z_{i}=1
numerical values become

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-0.7309\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e};

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,2)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\nu_{ei}}\Big[0.006587\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Y}}}^{(3,1)}_{e}+0.02041\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Y}}}^{(5,1)}_{e}+0.01647\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{e}}{\partial t}+0.1021\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{e}}{\partial t}\Big];

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-6.5455\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}\nu_{ei}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e};

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,2)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\nu_{ei}}\Big[1.0644\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Y}}}^{(3,1)}_{e}-0.2630\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Y}}}^{(5,1)}_{e}+2.6609\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{e}}{\partial t}-1.3152\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{e}}{\partial t}\Big];

(115)

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-3.1616\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{ei}}\nabla T_{e}+0.7110p_{e}\delta\boldsymbol{u};

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\nu_{ei}}\Big[5.3754\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{e}-0.9486\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{e}+0.9492\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}^{(1)}_{e}}{\partial t}-0.07906\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{e}}{\partial t}\Big];

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-110.664\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}m_{e}\nu_{ei}}\nabla T_{e}+18.7783\frac{p^{2}_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\delta\boldsymbol{u};

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,2)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\nu_{ei}}\Big[199.554\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}^{2}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{e}-34.831\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{e}+44.625\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}^{(1)}_{e}}{\partial t}-3.1747\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{e}}{\partial t}\Big].

(116)

By further applying the quasi-linear approximation, corrections to the electron viscosities become

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,2)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{25Z_{i}^{2}(119520Z_{i}^{2}+101784Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+46561)}{18D_{1}^{2}}\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}^{2}}\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e}}{\partial t}

(117)

\displaystyle-\frac{10Z_{i}^{2}(11040Z_{i}^{2}+15557Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+8922)}{D_{2}D_{1}}\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}\Big[\big(\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}\big)^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{25Z_{i}^{2}(534000Z_{i}^{2}+366451Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+131256)}{12D_{2}D_{1}}\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}^{2}m_{e}}\Big[2\nabla\nabla T_{e}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla^{2}T_{e}\Big];

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,2)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{175Z_{i}^{2}(173088Z_{i}^{2}+142032Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+59989)}{18D_{1}^{2}}\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{ei}^{2}\rho_{e}}\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e}}{\partial t}

(118)

\displaystyle-\frac{70Z_{i}^{2}(16992Z_{i}^{2}+23993Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+13698)}{D_{2}D_{1}}\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{ei}\rho_{e}}\Big[\big(\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}\big)^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{175Z_{i}^{2}(834576Z_{i}^{2}+603679Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+220824)}{12D_{2}D_{1}}\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{ei}^{2}m_{e}\rho_{e}}\Big[2\nabla\nabla T_{e}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla^{2}T_{e}\Big];

together with corrections for the heat fluxes

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{25Z_{i}^{2}(534000Z_{i}^{2}+366451Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+131256)}{12D_{1}D_{2}}\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}\nu_{ei}^{2}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e}

\displaystyle-\frac{75Z_{i}^{2}(5729Z_{i}^{2}+6711Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+4728)}{D_{2}^{2}}\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}\frac{\partial\delta\boldsymbol{u}}{\partial t}

\displaystyle+\frac{125Z_{i}^{2}(320797Z_{i}^{2}+202248Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+72864)}{8D_{2}^{2}}\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}^{2}m_{e}}\frac{\partial\nabla T_{e}}{\partial t};

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,2)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{175Z_{i}^{2}(712272Z_{i}^{2}+463249Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+155208)}{3D_{1}D_{2}}\frac{p_{e}^{3}}{\rho_{e}^{2}\nu_{ei}^{2}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e}

(119)

\displaystyle-\frac{2100Z_{i}^{2}(7611Z_{i}^{2}+8429Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+5000)}{D_{2}^{2}}\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{ei}\rho_{e}}\frac{\partial\delta\boldsymbol{u}}{\partial t}

\displaystyle+\frac{875Z_{i}^{2}(430783Z_{i}^{2}+261672Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+86880)}{2D_{2}^{2}}\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{ei}^{2}\rho_{e}m_{e}}\frac{\partial\nabla T_{e}}{\partial t}.

For the ion charge
Z_{i}=1
, the numerical values read

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,2)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+0.6801\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}^{2}}\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e}}{\partial t}-0.3880\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}\Big[\big(\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}\big)^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}\Big]

(120)

\displaystyle+2.2799\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}^{2}m_{e}}\Big[2\nabla\nabla T_{e}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla^{2}T_{e}\Big];

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,2)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+6.6638\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{ei}^{2}\rho_{e}}\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e}}{\partial t}-4.1827\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{ei}\rho_{e}}\Big[\big(\nabla\delta\boldsymbol{u}\big)^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}\Big]

(121)

\displaystyle+25.7440\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{ei}^{2}m_{e}\rho_{e}}\Big[2\nabla\nabla T_{e}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla^{2}T_{e}\Big];

together with

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2.2799\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}\nu_{ei}^{2}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e}-0.8098\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}\frac{\partial\delta\boldsymbol{u}}{\partial t}+5.7487\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}^{2}m_{e}}\frac{\partial\nabla T_{e}}{\partial t};

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,2)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 82.1278\frac{p_{e}^{3}}{\rho_{e}^{2}\nu_{ei}^{2}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e}-27.8859\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{ei}\rho_{e}}\frac{\partial\delta\boldsymbol{u}}{\partial t}+210.2318\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{ei}^{2}\rho_{e}m_{e}}\frac{\partial\nabla T_{e}}{\partial t}.

(122)

The rate-of-strain tensor
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e}
obviously enters the electron heat fluxes, even in a quasi-static approximation.

VI.3
Momentum exchange rates

Collisional momentum exchange rates
\boldsymbol{R}_{e}=-\boldsymbol{R}_{i}
given by (
48
)
can also be split into the first and second order

\boldsymbol{R}_{e}=\boldsymbol{R}_{e}^{(1)}+\boldsymbol{R}_{e}^{(2)}
, according to

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{R}_{e}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\rho_{e}\nu_{ei}\delta\boldsymbol{u}+\frac{21}{10}\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\nu_{ei}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}^{(1)}-\frac{3}{56}\frac{\rho_{e}^{2}}{p_{e}^{2}}\nu_{ei}\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{e};

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{R}_{e}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{21}{10}\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\nu_{ei}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}^{(2)}-\frac{3}{56}\frac{\rho_{e}^{2}}{p_{e}^{2}}\nu_{ei}\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,2)}_{e}.

(123)

Then by using results given in the previous section

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{R}_{e}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nu_{ei}\rho_{e}\frac{(D_{2}-153Z_{i}^{2}-360Z_{i}\sqrt{2})}{D_{2}}\delta\boldsymbol{u}-\frac{30Z_{i}(15\sqrt{2}+11Z_{i})}{D_{2}}n_{e}\nabla T_{e};

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{R}_{e}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{6Z_{i}(47\sqrt{2}+69Z_{i})}{D_{2}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{e}-\frac{6Z_{i}(11\sqrt{2}+13Z_{i})}{D_{2}}\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{e}

(124)

\displaystyle-\frac{12Z_{i}(29\sqrt{2}+4Z_{i})}{D_{2}}\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}^{(1)}_{e}}{\partial t}+\frac{3Z_{i}(2\sqrt{2}-Z_{i})}{D_{2}}\frac{\rho_{e}^{2}}{p_{e}^{2}}\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{e}}{\partial t},

or for a particular case of
Z_{i}=1

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{R}_{e}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+0.5980\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2,1)}_{e}-0.1261\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4,1)}_{e}

(125)

\displaystyle-0.3974\frac{\rho_{e}}{p_{e}}\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}^{(1)}_{e}}{\partial t}+0.004036\frac{\rho_{e}^{2}}{p_{e}^{2}}\frac{\partial\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5,1)}_{e}}{\partial t}.

Finally, at a semi-linear level

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{R}_{e}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{10Z_{i}^{2}(11040Z_{i}^{2}+15557Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+8922)}{D_{1}D_{2}}\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e}

(126)

\displaystyle-\frac{720Z_{i}^{2}(64Z_{i}^{2}+151Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+253)}{D_{2}^{2}}\rho_{e}\frac{\partial(\delta\boldsymbol{u})}{\partial t}

\displaystyle+\frac{75Z_{i}^{2}(5729Z_{i}^{2}+6711Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+4728)}{D_{2}^{2}}\frac{n_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}\frac{\partial\nabla T_{e}}{\partial t},

and for
Z_{i}=1
the full momentum exchange rates become

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{R}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-0.5129\nu_{ei}\rho_{e}\delta\boldsymbol{u}-0.7110n_{e}\nabla T_{e}+0.3880\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e}-0.2068\rho_{e}\frac{\partial(\delta\boldsymbol{u})}{\partial t}+0.8098\frac{n_{e}}{\nu_{ei}}\frac{\partial\nabla T_{e}}{\partial t},

(127)

where
\delta\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{u}_{e}-\boldsymbol{u}_{i}
.
Only the first two terms of (
127
) were considered by
Braginskii 1965
and
Spitzer & Härm 1953
(the latter having slightly
different proportionality constants; see Appendix
I
). A further generalization by keeping the full

\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}
&
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(4)}
viscosity tensors in the last terms of (
104
) & (
105
) brings another 3 terms to
\boldsymbol{R}_{e}

(not shown). Naturally, in a highly-collisional regime (
\nu_{ei}\gg\omega
) all additional terms are small in comparison to
the first two terms of (
127
).
Nevertheless, at higher frequencies
(shorter length-scales) these additional contributions might become significant. Interestingly,
the rate-of-strain tensor
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e}
enters the momentum exchange rates
(even at the linear level), with contribution
\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{e}=\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{e}+(1/3)\nabla(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{e})
.
Note that some terms are proportional to
1/\nu_{ei}
and become unbounded (divergent) in a regime of low-collisionality,
which is a consequence of the expansion procedure (i.e. a quasi-static approximation).
Evolution equations (
97
)-(
100
) are of course well-defined in the regime of low collisionality.

VII
Multi-fluid 22-moment model

Here we consider a natural generalization of the 21-moment model, by accounting for
a fully contracted perturbation of the 4th-order fluid moment
X_{ijkl}^{a(4)}=m_{a}\int c_{i}^{a}c_{j}^{a}c_{k}^{a}c_{l}^{a}f_{a}d^{3}v
. The fully contracted
(scalar) moment is decomposed into its Maxwellian core and a perturbation
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
(denoted with tilde), according to

X^{(4)}_{a}=m_{a}\int|\boldsymbol{c}_{a}|^{4}f_{a}d^{3}v=15\frac{p^{2}_{a}}{\rho_{a}}+\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a},

(128)

meaning a definition
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}=m_{a}\int|\boldsymbol{c}_{a}|^{4}(f_{a}-f_{a}^{(0)})d^{3}v
, where
f_{a}^{(0)}
is Maxwellian. The scalar
perturbation
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
enters the decomposition of the 4th-order moment

\displaystyle X_{ijkl}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{15}\Big(15\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}+\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}\Big)\big(\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk}\big)

(129)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{7}\Big[\Pi_{ij}^{a(4)}\delta_{kl}+\Pi_{ik}^{a(4)}\delta_{jl}+\Pi_{il}^{a(4)}\delta_{jk}+\Pi_{jk}^{a(4)}\delta_{il}+\Pi_{jl}^{a(4)}\delta_{ik}+\Pi_{kl}^{a(4)}\delta_{ij}\Big]+\sigma_{ijkl}^{a(4)}\,{}^{\prime},

where we neglect the traceless tensor
\sigma_{ijkl}^{a(4)}\,{}^{\prime}
,
and the entire model now represents the 22-moment model.
The fully non-linear model is given by evolution equations (
6
)-(
9
) which are unchanged,
together with

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}}{dt}+\frac{7}{5}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{a}+\frac{7}{5}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{a}+\frac{2}{5}(\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{a})\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}+\frac{5}{2}p_{a}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}\nabla\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}+\frac{1}{2}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-\frac{5}{2}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}-\frac{1}{\rho_{a}}(\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{a})\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}

\displaystyle\qquad=\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(3)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(3)}_{a}-\frac{5}{2}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\boldsymbol{R}_{a}-\frac{1}{\rho_{a}}\boldsymbol{R}_{a}\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a};

(130)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}+\frac{1}{5}\Big[(\nabla\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a})^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}(\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a})\Big]+\frac{9}{7}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{a})\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}+\frac{9}{7}(\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{a})^{S}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{7}\big((\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{a})\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}\big)^{S}-\frac{22}{21}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}(\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}:\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{a})-\,\frac{14}{5\rho_{a}}\Big[\big((\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{a})\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\big)^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}(\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{a})\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\Big]

\displaystyle+\Omega_{a}\big(\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}\big)^{S}+\frac{7}{15}\big(15\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}+\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}\big)\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{a}

\displaystyle=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}\equiv\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(4)}_{a}-\frac{\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}}{3}\textrm{Tr}\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(4)}_{a}-\frac{14}{5\rho_{a}}\Big[(\boldsymbol{R}_{a}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a})^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}(\boldsymbol{R}_{a}\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a})\Big];

(131)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}+\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-20\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}+\frac{7}{3}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{a})+4\big(\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-5\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\big):\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{a}

\displaystyle-\frac{8}{\rho_{a}}(\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{a})\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}=\widetilde{Q}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}\equiv\textrm{Tr}\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(4)}_{a}-20\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}Q_{a}-\frac{8}{\rho_{a}}\boldsymbol{R}_{a}\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a};

(132)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+\frac{1}{3}\nabla\widetilde{X}^{(6)}_{a}+\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(6)}_{a}

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{5}\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}(\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{a})+\frac{9}{5}\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{a}+\frac{4}{5}(\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{a})\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}

\displaystyle+70\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)-35\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}^{2}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}-\frac{7}{3\rho_{a}}\big(\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{a}\big)\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}-\frac{4}{\rho_{a}}\big(\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{a}\big)\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}

\displaystyle=\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(5)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}\equiv\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(5)}_{a}-35\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}^{2}}\boldsymbol{R}_{a}-\frac{7}{3\rho_{a}}\boldsymbol{R}_{a}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}-\frac{4}{\rho_{a}}\boldsymbol{R}_{a}\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}.

(133)

The last equation (
133
) is closed with closure (
13
) for the stress-tensor
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(6)}_{a}
,
together with a closure for the scalar perturbation (derived from a Hermite closure)

\widetilde{X}^{(6)}_{a}=m_{a}\int|\boldsymbol{c}_{a}|^{6}(f_{a}-f_{a}^{(0)})d^{3}v=21\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}.

(134)

In the semi-linear approximations the 22-moment model reads

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}\big)^{S}+p_{a}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{a}+\frac{2}{5}\Big((\nabla\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a})^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}_{a}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime};

(135)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}+\frac{5}{2}p_{a}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-\frac{5}{2}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{6}\nabla\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}=\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(3)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime};

(136)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\big(\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}\big)^{S}+7\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{a}+\frac{1}{5}\Big[(\nabla\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a})^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}(\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a})\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime};

(137)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}+\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-20\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}=\widetilde{Q}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}=\textrm{Tr}\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(4)}_{a}-20\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}Q_{a};

(138)

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}+70\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)+18\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-98\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}^{2}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}

\displaystyle\qquad+7\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}=\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(5)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}.

(139)

As discussed in Section
II.4
, in the semi-linear approximation we are neglecting terms such as
(\nabla p_{a})\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}

which might become significant in the presence of large-scale gradients, together with other terms that are neglected.
In comparison to the 21-moment model given by (
97
)-(
100
), evolution equations
(
135
) & (
137
) for stress-tensors
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}
&
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}
remain unchanged.
Importantly, collisional contributions
\boldsymbol{R}_{a}
;

\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}_{a}^{(2)}\,{}^{\prime}
;
\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(3)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}
;
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}_{a}^{(4)}\,{}^{\prime}
;
\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(5)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}

given in Section
II.1
remain unchanged as well. The only differences are:
1) scalar perturbations
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
now enter the left hand sides of evolution equations (
136
) & (
139
) for
heat fluxes
\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}
&
\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}
; 2) a new evolution equation (
138
) for scalar
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
is present, with
collisional contributions
\widetilde{Q}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}
that need to be specified;
3) the energy exchange rates
Q_{a}
entering the scalar pressure equation (
8
) are modified, and given below.

VII.1
Collisional contributions (arbitrary temperatures)

The energy exchange rates entering equation (
8
) are now given by

\displaystyle Q_{a}=\sum_{b\neq a}Q_{ab}=\sum_{b\neq a}\frac{\rho_{a}\nu_{ab}}{(m_{a}+m_{b})}\Big\{3(T_{b}-T_{a})+\hat{P}_{ab(1)}\frac{\rho_{a}}{n_{a}p_{a}}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}-\hat{P}_{ab(2)}\frac{\rho_{b}}{n_{b}p_{b}}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{b}\Big\},

(140)

with mass-ratio coefficients

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{ab(1)}=\frac{3T_{a}m_{b}(5T_{b}m_{b}+4T_{b}m_{a}-T_{a}m_{b})}{40(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};\qquad\hat{P}_{ab(2)}=\frac{3T_{b}m_{a}(5T_{a}m_{a}+4T_{a}m_{b}-T_{b}m_{a})}{40(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}}.

(141)

Interestingly, scalar perturbations
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
thus enter the energy exchange rates. For self-collisions all the contributions
naturally disappear. As discussed also later in Section
VIII.1
, for multi-fluid models the conservation of energy

Q_{ab}+Q_{ba}=(\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a})\cdot\boldsymbol{R}_{ab}
is satisfied only approximately, because in the semi-linear approximation the differences in
drifts
\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a}
are assumed to be small, meaning
Q_{ab}+Q_{ba}=0
holds.
To satisfy the energy conservation exactly, the collisional integrals would have to be calculated non-linearly with unrestriced drifts (i.e. with the runaway effect).
Nevertheless, for a plasma consisting of only two species
(such as a one ion-electron plasma) the conservation of energy can be imposed by hand, by calculating
Q_{ab}

according to (
140
), (
141
) and prescribing
Q_{ba}=-Q_{ab}+(\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a})\cdot\boldsymbol{R}_{ab}
.

Collisional exchange rates entering evolution equation (
138
) are given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{4}{5}\nu_{aa}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}+\sum_{b\neq a}\nu_{ab}\Big\{-\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\frac{(T_{b}-T_{a})}{T_{a}}\hat{S}_{ab(0)}-\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}\hat{S}_{ab(1)}+\frac{p_{a}^{2}\rho_{b}}{p_{b}^{2}\rho_{a}}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{b}\hat{S}_{ab(2)}\Big\},

(142)

with mass-ratio coefficients

\displaystyle\hat{S}_{ab(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{36T_{a}m_{a}m_{b}}{(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})(m_{b}+m_{a})};

\displaystyle\hat{S}_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\Big\{m_{a}\big(17T_{a}^{3}m_{b}^{3}-36T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}-69T_{a}^{2}T_{b}m_{b}^{3}+12T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}-48T_{a}T_{b}^{2}m_{a}m_{b}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-40T_{b}^{3}m_{a}^{3}-84T_{b}^{3}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}\big)\Big\}\Big[10(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}(m_{b}+m_{a})\Big]^{-1};

\displaystyle\hat{S}_{ab(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3T_{b}^{2}m_{a}^{2}m_{b}(7T_{a}m_{a}+4T_{a}m_{b}-3T_{b}m_{a})}{2(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{3}(m_{b}+m_{a})},

(143)

where the self-collisional contributions are represented by the first term of (
142
).

VII.1.1
Small temperature differences

For small temperature differences the mass-ratio coefficients become

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{ab(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3m_{b}}{10(m_{b}+m_{a})};\qquad\hat{P}_{ab(2)}=\frac{3m_{a}}{10(m_{b}+m_{a})};

\displaystyle\hat{S}_{ab(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{36m_{a}m_{b}}{(m_{b}+m_{a})^{2}};\qquad\hat{S}_{ab(1)}=\frac{2m_{a}(10m_{a}^{2}+8m_{a}m_{b}+13m_{b}^{2})}{5(m_{b}+m_{a})^{3}};\qquad\hat{S}_{ab(2)}=\frac{6m_{a}^{2}m_{b}}{(m_{b}+m_{a})^{3}},

(144)

and for example for self-collisions
\hat{S}_{aa(1)}=31/20
and
\hat{S}_{aa(2)}=3/4
.
We further consider a one ion-electron plasma.

VII.2
Ion species (self-collisions)

In a quasi-static approximation the solution of equation (
138
) becomes

\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}=-\,\frac{5}{4\nu_{aa}}\Big[\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-20\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\Big].

(145)

The quasi-static solution is thus completely determined by the heat fluxes
\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}
&
\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}
and for a magnetized plasma it has
the following form

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{5}{4\nu_{aa}}\Big\{\nabla\cdot\Big[\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big(-\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(5)}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{a}-\kappa_{\perp}^{a(5)}\nabla_{\perp}T_{a}+\kappa_{\times}^{a(5)}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{a}\Big)\Big]

(146)

\displaystyle\quad-20\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\Big(-\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{a}-\kappa_{\perp}^{a}\nabla_{\perp}T_{a}+\kappa_{\times}^{a}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{a}\Big)\Big\},

where the thermal conductivities are given by (
43
), (
46
).

It feels natural to define thermal conductivities (of the moment
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
)

\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(4)}=\frac{5}{4}\big(\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(5)}-20\kappa_{\parallel}^{a}\big);\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{a(4)}=\frac{5}{4}\big(\kappa_{\perp}^{a(5)}-20\kappa_{\perp}^{a}\big);\qquad\kappa_{\times}^{a(4)}=\frac{5}{4}\big(\kappa_{\times}^{a(5)}-20\kappa_{\times}^{a}\big),

(147)

and result (
146
) then transforms into

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\rho_{a}}\nabla\cdot\Big[-\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(4)}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{a}-\kappa_{\perp}^{a(4)}\nabla_{\perp}T_{a}+\kappa_{\times}^{a(4)}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{a}\Big]

(148)

\displaystyle-\frac{5}{4\nu_{aa}}\Big(-\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(5)}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{a}-\kappa_{\perp}^{a(5)}\nabla_{\perp}T_{a}+\kappa_{\times}^{a(5)}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{a}\Big)\cdot\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big),

with thermal conductivities

\displaystyle{\kappa}_{\parallel}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1375}{24}\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}};

\displaystyle{\kappa}_{\perp}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}}\frac{5x^{2}+(9504/245)}{x^{4}+(3313/1225)x^{2}+(20736/30625)};

\displaystyle{\kappa}_{\times}^{a(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}m_{a}}\frac{25x^{3}+(3810/49)x}{x^{4}+(3313/1225)x^{2}+(20736/30625)}.

(149)

The second term of (
148
) is strictly non-linear and may be neglected for simplicity. The solution
for
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}
thus can be written as a divergence of a heat flux vector defined by the expression in the
square brackets of (
148
). We have used Braginskii notation with vectors

\nabla_{\parallel}=\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\cdot\nabla
and
\nabla_{\perp}=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}_{\perp}\cdot\nabla=-\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\nabla
.

The result (
148
) can be further simplified in the semi-linear approximation, where one may use

\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{a})=0
and so

\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}=+\frac{p_{a}}{\nu_{aa}\rho_{a}}\Big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{a(4)}\nabla_{\parallel}^{2}T_{a}+\kappa_{\perp}^{a(4)}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}T_{a}\Big],

(150)

with scalars
\nabla_{\parallel}^{2}=\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}:\nabla\nabla
and
\nabla_{\perp}^{2}=\nabla^{2}-\nabla_{\parallel}^{2}
, and for
zero magnetic field

\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}=+\underbrace{\frac{1375}{24}}_{57.292}\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\nu_{aa}^{2}\rho_{a}m_{a}}\nabla^{2}T_{a}.

(151)

Note that the result is proportional to
1/\nu_{aa}^{2}
and thus small in a highly-collisional regime.

VII.3
Electron species (one ion-electron plasma)

Here we consider a one ion-electron plasma with small temperature differences. Similar to Braginskii, an exact energy conservation can be imposed by
hand, according to

\displaystyle Q_{ie}=\frac{\rho_{i}\nu_{ie}}{m_{i}}\Big[3(T_{e}-T_{i})+\frac{3}{10}m_{e}\Big(\frac{\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{i}}{p_{i}}-\frac{\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{e}}{p_{e}}\Big)\Big];\qquad Q_{ei}=-Q_{ie}-(\boldsymbol{u}_{e}-\boldsymbol{u}_{i})\cdot\boldsymbol{R}_{ei}.

(152)

The electron coefficients (
144
) become

\hat{S}_{ei(1)}=(26/5)(m_{e}/m_{i})
and
\hat{S}_{ei(2)}=6(m_{e}/m_{i})^{2}
, and collisional contributions
(
142
) have a simple form

\widetilde{Q}^{(4)}_{e}\,{}^{\prime}=-\frac{4}{5}\nu_{ee}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{e},

(153)

determined solely by the electron-electron collisions. A quasi-static solution of equation (
138
) then becomes

\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{e}=-\,\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4\nu_{ei}}\Big[\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{e}-20\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{e}\Big],

(154)

where we have used
\nu_{ee}=\nu_{ei}/(Z_{i}\sqrt{2})
. The electron heat fluxes are given by (
53
) and (
61
) and are of course determined by
both electron-electron and electron-ion collisions. The full solution thus consists of thermal and frictional parts

\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{e}=\widetilde{X}^{(4)T}_{e}+\widetilde{X}^{(4)u}_{e}
, where

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{(4)T}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4\nu_{ei}}\Big\{\nabla\cdot\Big[\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\Big(-\kappa_{\parallel}^{e(5)}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{e}-\kappa_{\perp}^{e(5)}\nabla_{\perp}T_{e}+\kappa_{\times}^{e(5)}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{e}\Big)\Big]

(155)

\displaystyle\quad-20\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\nabla\cdot\Big(-\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{e}-\kappa_{\perp}^{e}\nabla_{\perp}T_{e}+\kappa_{\times}^{e}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{e}\Big)\Big\};

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{(4)u}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{5Z_{i}\sqrt{2}}{4\nu_{ei}}\Big\{\nabla\cdot\Big[\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\rho_{e}}\Big(\beta_{0}^{(5)}\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{\parallel}+\frac{\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime}}x^{2}+\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime}}}{\triangle}\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{\perp}-\frac{\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}x^{3}+\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}x}{\triangle}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\delta\boldsymbol{u}\Big)\Big]

(156)

\displaystyle\quad-20\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\nabla\cdot\Big(\beta_{0}p_{e}\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{\parallel}+p_{e}\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{\perp}\frac{\beta_{1}^{\prime}x^{2}+\beta_{0}^{\prime}}{\triangle}-p_{e}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\delta\boldsymbol{u}\frac{\beta_{1}^{\prime\prime}x^{3}+\beta_{0}^{\prime\prime}x}{\triangle}\Big)\Big\},

with
\delta\boldsymbol{u}=\boldsymbol{u}_{e}-\boldsymbol{u}_{i}
. It is again natural to define electron thermal conductivities
(of the moment
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{e}
)

\kappa_{\parallel}^{e(4)}=\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4}\big(\kappa_{\parallel}^{e(5)}-20\kappa_{\parallel}^{e}\big);\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{e(4)}=\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4}\big(\kappa_{\perp}^{e(5)}-20\kappa_{\perp}^{e}\big);\qquad\kappa_{\times}^{e(4)}=\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4}\big(\kappa_{\times}^{e(5)}-20\kappa_{\times}^{e}\big),

(157)

together with transport coefficients

\displaystyle\beta_{0}^{(4)}=\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4}\big(\beta_{0}^{(5)}-20\beta_{0}\big);\qquad\beta_{1}^{(4)^{\prime}}=\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4}\big(\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime}}-20\beta_{1}^{\prime}\big);\qquad\beta_{0}^{(4)^{\prime}}=\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4}\big(\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime}}-20\beta_{0}^{\prime}\big);

\displaystyle\beta_{1}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4}\big(\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}-20\beta_{1}^{\prime\prime}\big);\qquad\beta_{0}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4}\big(\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}-20\beta_{0}^{\prime\prime}\big);

(158)

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{(4)}=\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4}\big(\gamma_{0}^{(5)}-20\gamma_{0}\big);\qquad\gamma_{1}^{(4)^{\prime}}=\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4}\big(\gamma_{1}^{(5)^{\prime}}-20\gamma_{1}^{\prime}\big);\qquad\gamma_{0}^{(4)^{\prime}}=\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4}\big(\gamma_{0}^{(5)^{\prime}}-20\gamma_{0}^{\prime}\big);

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4}\big(\gamma_{1}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}-20\gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}\big);\qquad\gamma_{0}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4}\big(\gamma_{0}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}-20\gamma_{0}^{\prime\prime}\big).

(159)

The thermal and frictional parts then become

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{(4)T}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}\rho_{e}}\nabla\cdot\Big(-\kappa_{\parallel}^{e(4)}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{e}-\kappa_{\perp}^{e(4)}\nabla_{\perp}T_{e}+\kappa_{\times}^{e(4)}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{e}\Big)

(160)

\displaystyle-\,\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4\nu_{ei}}\Big(-\kappa_{\parallel}^{e(5)}\nabla_{\parallel}T_{e}-\kappa_{\perp}^{e(5)}\nabla_{\perp}T_{e}+\kappa_{\times}^{e(5)}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\nabla T_{e}\Big)\cdot\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\Big);

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{(4)u}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}\rho_{e}}\nabla\cdot\Big(\beta_{0}^{(4)}p_{e}\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{\parallel}+\frac{\beta_{1}^{(4)^{\prime}}x^{2}+\beta_{0}^{(4)^{\prime}}}{\triangle}p_{e}\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{\perp}-\frac{\beta_{1}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}x^{3}+\beta_{0}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}x}{\triangle}p_{e}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\delta\boldsymbol{u}\Big)

(161)

\displaystyle-\,\frac{5\sqrt{2}Z_{i}}{4\nu_{ei}}\nabla\cdot\Big(\beta_{0}^{(5)}p_{e}\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{\parallel}+\frac{\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime}}x^{2}+\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime}}}{\triangle}p_{e}\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{\perp}-\frac{\beta_{1}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}x^{3}+\beta_{0}^{(5)^{\prime\prime}}x}{\triangle}p_{e}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\delta\boldsymbol{u}\Big)\cdot\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{e}}{\rho_{e}}\Big),

where the second terms of (
160
) & (
161
) are purely non-linear and may be neglected for simplicity.
The thermal conductivities are

\kappa_{\parallel}^{e(4)}=\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{ei}}\gamma_{0}^{(4)};\qquad\kappa_{\perp}^{e(4)}=\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{ei}}\frac{\gamma_{1}^{(4)^{\prime}}x^{2}+\gamma_{0}^{(4)^{\prime}}}{\triangle};\qquad\kappa_{\times}^{e(4)}=\frac{p_{e}}{m_{e}\nu_{ei}}\frac{\gamma_{1}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}x^{3}+\gamma_{0}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}x}{\triangle},

(162)

and the transport coefficients become

\displaystyle\beta_{0}^{(4)}=\frac{150Z_{i}^{2}\sqrt{2}(16\sqrt{2}+29Z_{i})}{217Z_{i}^{2}+604Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+288};\qquad\beta_{1}^{(4)^{\prime}}=-\,\frac{3\sqrt{2}(548\sqrt{2}+1261Z_{i})}{224};

\displaystyle\beta_{0}^{(4)^{\prime}}=\frac{3\sqrt{2}(217Z_{i}^{2}+604Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+288)(16\sqrt{2}+29Z_{i})}{9800Z_{i}^{2}};

\displaystyle\beta_{1}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}=-\,\frac{15Z_{i}\sqrt{2}}{4};\qquad\beta_{0}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{3\sqrt{2}(3079Z_{i}^{2}+3181Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+1420)}{490Z_{i}};

(163)

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{(4)}=\frac{250Z_{i}^{2}\sqrt{2}(66\sqrt{2}+229Z_{i})}{217Z_{i}^{2}+604Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+288};\qquad\gamma_{1}^{(4)^{\prime}}=\frac{5\sqrt{2}(4\sqrt{2}-17Z_{i})}{8};

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{(4)^{\prime}}=\frac{\sqrt{2}(217Z_{i}^{2}+604Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+288)(66\sqrt{2}+229Z_{i})}{1960Z_{i}^{2}};

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}=25Z_{i}\sqrt{2};\qquad\gamma_{0}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{\sqrt{2}(176437Z_{i}^{2}+102558Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+30480)}{784Z_{i}};

(164)

\displaystyle\triangle=x^{4}+\delta_{1}x^{2}+\delta_{0};

\displaystyle\delta_{0}=\Big(\frac{217Z_{i}^{2}+604Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+288}{700Z_{i}^{2}}\Big)^{2};\qquad\delta_{1}=\frac{586601Z_{i}^{2}+330152Z_{i}\sqrt{2}+106016}{78400Z_{i}^{2}},

(165)

and with numerical values for
Z_{i}=1

\displaystyle\beta_{0}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 8.0576;\qquad\beta_{1}^{(4)^{\prime}}=-38.5624;\qquad\beta_{0}^{(4)^{\prime}}=30.3787;\qquad\beta_{1}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}=-5.3033;\qquad\beta_{0}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}=77.9054;

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 83.8471;\qquad\gamma_{1}^{(4)^{\prime}}=-10.0260;\qquad\gamma_{0}^{(4)^{\prime}}=316.1179;\qquad\gamma_{1}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}=35.3553;\qquad\gamma_{0}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}=634.8735;

\displaystyle\delta_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3.7702;\qquad\delta_{1}=14.7898.

(166)

At the semi-linear level the solution becomes

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{(4)T}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{p_{e}}{\nu_{ei}\rho_{e}}\Big[\kappa_{\parallel}^{e(4)}\nabla_{\parallel}^{2}T_{e}+\kappa_{\perp}^{e(4)}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}T_{e}\Big];

(167)

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{(4)u}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{ei}\rho_{e}}\Big[\beta_{0}^{(4)}\nabla\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{\parallel}+\frac{\beta_{1}^{(4)^{\prime}}x^{2}+\beta_{0}^{(4)^{\prime}}}{\triangle}\nabla\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}_{\perp}-\frac{\beta_{1}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}x^{3}+\beta_{0}^{(4)^{\prime\prime}}x}{\triangle}\nabla\cdot(\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\delta\boldsymbol{u})\Big],

(168)

and for zero magnetic field

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{(4)}\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{ei}^{2}\rho_{e}m_{e}}\nabla^{2}T_{e}-\beta_{0}^{(4)}\frac{p_{e}^{2}}{\nu_{ei}\rho_{e}}\nabla\cdot\delta\boldsymbol{u}.

(169)

VIII
Discussion & Conclusions

Here we discuss various topics that we find of importance.

VIII.1
Energy conservation

Collisional integrals were calculated in a
semi-linear approximation, where all quantities such as
\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\cdot(\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a})
or
|\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a}|^{2}
are neglected and considered small.
This approach is typically used for calculations with Landau or Boltzmann collisional operators, and
is for example used in the models of
Burgers 1969
and
Schunk 1977
.
Importantly, an exact energy conservation
Q_{ab}+Q_{ba}=(\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a})\cdot\boldsymbol{R}_{ab}
cannot be achieved, because
the collisional integrals would have to be calculated non-linearly.
An exact conservation of energy can be achieved only
in two particular cases, the first being a one ion-electron plasma (or a two-species plasma) where the conservation of energy can be imposed by hand,
according to

Q_{ie}=3n_{e}\nu_{ei}(T_{e}-T_{i})\frac{m_{e}}{m_{i}};\qquad Q_{ei}=-Q_{ie}-(\boldsymbol{u}_{e}-\boldsymbol{u}_{i})\cdot\boldsymbol{R}_{ei},

(170)

which is the choice of
Braginskii 1965
; see his equation (2.18).
Such a construction cannot be done in general for multi-species plasmas, and conservation of energy is thus satisfied only approximately.

The second particular case is by neglecting all heat fluxes and stress-tensors, and considering only
a 5-moment model with perturbation
\chi_{a}=0
.
In this specific example of collisions between strict Maxwellians, multi-fluid calculations can be done for unrestricted
drifts (see
Burgers 1969
,
Schunk 1977
, and our Appendix
G.3
), yielding

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{R}_{ab}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho_{a}\nu_{ab}(\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a})\Phi_{ab};

(171)

\displaystyle Q_{ab}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho_{a}\nu_{ab}\Big[3\frac{T_{b}-T_{a}}{m_{a}+m_{b}}\Psi_{ab}+\frac{m_{b}}{m_{a}+m_{b}}|\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a}|^{2}\Phi_{ab}\Big],

(172)

where one defines functions

\displaystyle\Psi_{ab}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-\epsilon^{2}};\qquad\Phi_{ab}=\Big(\frac{3}{4}\sqrt{\pi}\frac{\textrm{erf}(\epsilon)}{\epsilon^{3}}-\frac{3}{2}\frac{e^{-\epsilon^{2}}}{\epsilon^{2}}\Big);\qquad\epsilon=\frac{|\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a}|}{\sqrt{v_{\textrm{th}a}^{2}+v_{\textrm{th}b}^{2}}},

(173)

thermal velocities
v_{\textrm{th}a}^{2}=2T_{a}/m_{a}
, and collisional frequencies (
178
).
Because
\rho_{a}\nu_{ab}=\rho_{b}\nu_{ba}
holds, both momentum and energy are conserved. Collisional exchange rates (
171
), (
172
)
represent the “runaway” effect, and the function
\Phi_{ab}
is directly related to the Chandrasekhar function; for further
details see Appendix
G.3
and Figure
6
.

For a particular case when differences in drift velocities
|\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a}|
become much smaller than thermal velocities so that
\epsilon\ll 1
, functions

\Phi_{ab}\to 1
and
\Psi_{ab}\to 1
and
\boldsymbol{R}_{ab}=\rho_{a}\nu_{ab}(\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a})
.
To correctly account for small
|\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a}|^{2}
contributions in
Q_{ab}
while keeping the differences in temperatures unrestricted
is achieved by
\Psi_{ab}=1-\epsilon^{2}
, yielding the following equivalent forms

\displaystyle Q_{ab}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho_{a}\nu_{ab}\Big[3\frac{T_{b}-T_{a}}{m_{a}+m_{b}}\Big(1-\frac{|\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a}|^{2}}{\frac{2T_{a}}{m_{a}}+\frac{2T_{b}}{m_{b}}}\Big)+\frac{m_{b}}{m_{b}+m_{a}}|\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a}|^{2}\Big];

(174)

\displaystyle Q_{ab}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho_{a}\nu_{ab}\Big[3\frac{T_{b}-T_{a}}{m_{a}+m_{b}}+\frac{3}{2}\Big(\frac{T_{a}m_{b}}{T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a}}-\frac{1}{3}\frac{m_{b}}{m_{b}+m_{a}}\Big)|\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a}|^{2}\Big];

(175)

see also (
G32
).
Energy is still conserved. When additionally the differences in temperatures are small as well
(with respect to their mean temperature), the frictional part simplifies into

Q_{ab}=\rho_{a}\nu_{ab}\Big[3\frac{T_{b}-T_{a}}{m_{a}+m_{b}}+\frac{m_{b}}{m_{a}+m_{b}}|\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a}|^{2}\Big].

(176)

One can of course neglect the runaway effect from the beginning, and account for small
|\boldsymbol{u}_{b}-\boldsymbol{u}_{a}|^{2}
contributions
either through the center-of-mass velocity transformation, as is for example done in the Appendix of
Braginskii 1965
,
or by using the Rosenbluth potentials; see Appendix
G.1
,
G.2
.

Note that considering the 22-moment model, the fully contracted scalar perturbations
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}

modify the energy conservation, according to

\displaystyle Q_{ab}=\frac{\rho_{a}\nu_{ab}}{(m_{a}+m_{b})}\Big[3(T_{b}-T_{a})+\hat{P}_{ab(1)}\frac{\rho_{a}}{n_{a}p_{a}}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}-\hat{P}_{ab(2)}\frac{\rho_{b}}{n_{b}p_{b}}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{b}\Big];

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{ab(1)}=\frac{3T_{a}m_{b}(5T_{b}m_{b}+4T_{b}m_{a}-T_{a}m_{b})}{40(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}};\qquad\hat{P}_{ab(2)}=\frac{3T_{b}m_{a}(5T_{a}m_{a}+4T_{a}m_{b}-T_{b}m_{a})}{40(T_{a}m_{b}+T_{b}m_{a})^{2}},

(177)

and for only two species one can again impose an exact energy conservation by hand; see e.g. (
152
).

VIII.2
Collisional frequencies for ion-electron plasma

The Landau collisional operator yields the following collisional frequencies (see for example
Hinton 1983
or our Appendix
G.1
)

\nu_{ab}=\tau^{-1}_{ab}=\frac{16}{3}\sqrt{\pi}\frac{n_{b}e^{4}Z_{a}^{2}Z_{b}^{2}\ln\Lambda}{m_{a}^{2}(v_{\textrm{th}a}^{2}+v_{\textrm{th}b}^{2})^{3/2}}\Big(1+\frac{m_{a}}{m_{b}}\Big),

(178)

where
v_{\textrm{th}a}^{2}=2T_{a}/m_{a}
, and
\rho_{a}\nu_{ab}=\rho_{b}\nu_{ba}
holds.
Equivalently, in the form of
Burgers 1969
&
Schunk 1977

\nu_{ab}=\frac{16}{3}\sqrt{\pi}\Big(\frac{\mu_{ab}}{2T_{ab}}\Big)^{3/2}\frac{m_{b}n_{b}}{m_{a}+m_{b}}\frac{e^{4}Z_{a}^{2}Z_{b}^{2}\ln\Lambda}{\mu_{ab}^{2}},

(179)

where the reduced mass
\mu_{ab}
and reduced temperature
T_{ab}
are defined in (
14
).
For a particular case of self-collisions

\nu_{aa}=\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{\pi}\frac{n_{a}e^{4}Z_{a}^{4}\ln\Lambda}{T_{a}^{3/2}\sqrt{m_{a}}}.

(180)

For a particular case of
T_{a}=T_{b}=T
,

\nu_{ab}=\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{2\pi}\frac{n_{b}e^{4}Z_{a}^{2}Z_{b}^{2}\ln\Lambda}{T^{3/2}}\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{ab}}}{m_{a}},

(181)

which identifies with equation (7.6) of
Braginskii 1965
(after one uses
\nu_{ab}=n_{b}\mu_{ab}\alpha_{ab}^{\prime}/m_{a}
).
For a particular case of a one ion-electron plasma, collisional frequencies simplify into

\displaystyle\nu_{ii}=\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{\pi}\frac{n_{i}e^{4}Z_{i}^{4}\ln\Lambda}{T^{3/2}_{i}\sqrt{m_{i}}};\qquad\nu_{ie}=\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{2\pi}\frac{n_{e}e^{4}Z_{i}^{2}\ln\Lambda}{T^{3/2}_{e}\sqrt{m_{i}}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{e}}{m_{i}}};

\displaystyle\nu_{ee}=\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{\pi}\frac{n_{e}e^{4}\ln\Lambda}{T^{3/2}_{e}\sqrt{m_{e}}};\qquad\nu_{ei}=\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{2\pi}\frac{n_{i}e^{4}Z_{i}^{2}\ln\Lambda}{T^{3/2}_{e}\sqrt{m_{e}}},

(182)

where one assumes
T_{i}/m_{i}\ll T_{e}/m_{e}
, so the ions cannot be extremely hot.
Obviously,
\nu_{ii}\gg\nu_{ie}
(by a factor of
\sqrt{m_{i}/m_{e}}
for equal temperatures and
Z_{i}=1
),
however
\nu_{ee}\sim\nu_{ei}
, with exact relation
\nu_{ei}=Z_{i}\sqrt{2}\nu_{ee}
after one uses
n_{e}=Z_{i}n_{i}
.
The relation
\rho_{i}\nu_{ie}=\rho_{e}\nu_{ei}
holds exactly in (
182
). Note the important difference
that while
\nu_{ei}
contains a factor of
\sqrt{2}
, the
\nu_{ii}
does not.
Thus, comparing
Braginskii 1965
expressions (2.5i) and (2.5e) with definitions (
182
), Braginskii clearly uses

\tau_{i}=\tau_{ii};\qquad\tau_{e}=\tau_{ei},

(183)

which also agrees with his definition (7.6), equivalent to our (
181
).

However, very often when considering ion-electron plasma, a different definition of
\nu_{ab}
is used without the reduced mass,
in the following form

\displaystyle m_{a}\ll m_{b}:\qquad\nu_{ab}=\tau_{ab}^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{16}{3}\sqrt{\pi}\frac{n_{b}e^{4}Z_{a}^{2}Z_{b}^{2}\ln\Lambda}{m_{a}^{2}v_{\textrm{th}a}^{3}}=\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{2\pi}\frac{n_{b}e^{4}Z_{a}^{2}Z_{b}^{2}\ln\Lambda}{T_{a}^{3/2}\sqrt{m_{a}}},

(184)

which for example agrees with the Appendix of
Helander & Sigmar 2002
(page 277, after using cgs units
\epsilon_{0}\to 1/(4\pi)
).
We have added the
m_{a}\ll m_{b}
designation even though it is not present
in
Helander & Sigmar 2002
, because obviously it is the only way how to obtain (
184
) from the general
(
178
).
Importantly,
\rho_{a}\nu_{ab}\neq\rho_{b}\nu_{ba}
, and if one would use (
184
) to calculate
\nu_{ie}
,
the result would be erroneous. Instead, the
\nu_{ie}
must be
calculated from
\nu_{ei}
so that the momentum is conserved. Technically, (
184
) should not be used for self-collisions either.
Nevertheless, using (
184
) yields the following collisional frequencies

\displaystyle\nu_{ii}=\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{2\pi}\frac{n_{i}e^{4}Z_{i}^{4}\ln\Lambda}{T^{3/2}_{i}\sqrt{m_{i}}};\qquad\nu_{ie}=\frac{m_{e}n_{e}}{m_{i}n_{i}}\nu_{ei}=\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{2\pi}\frac{n_{e}e^{4}Z_{i}^{2}\ln\Lambda}{T^{3/2}_{e}\sqrt{m_{i}}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{e}}{m_{i}}};

\displaystyle\nu_{ee}=\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{2\pi}\frac{n_{e}e^{4}\ln\Lambda}{T^{3/2}_{e}\sqrt{m_{e}}};\qquad\nu_{ei}=\frac{4}{3}\sqrt{2\pi}\frac{n_{i}e^{4}Z_{i}^{2}\ln\Lambda}{T^{3/2}_{e}\sqrt{m_{e}}}.

(185)

Now
\nu_{ii}
contains a factor of
\sqrt{2}
, leading to an interpretation that Braginskii uses

\tau_{i}=\sqrt{2}\tau_{ii};\qquad\tau_{e}=\tau_{ei}.

(186)

Also, now for
Z_{i}=1
relation
\nu_{ee}=\nu_{ei}
holds. These definitions of collisional frequencies are
used in a majority of the modern plasma literature, where one argues that it seems unnatural to introduce asymmetry between

\nu_{ii}
and
\nu_{ei}
(see e.g. Part 1 of
Balescu 1988
, p.192, p.274).
Obviously, for multi-species
plasmas collisional frequencies (
178
) have to be used, and we thus find it much more natural to use
the original
Braginskii 1965
definitions (
182
), (
183
) also for an ion-electron plasma.
Of course, for the Landau operator both approaches yield the same results because the collisional integrals are properly
calculated. However, a difference arises for the phenomenological operators such as the BGK or the Dougherty (Lenard-Bernstein) operators,
where for example one needs to add
\nu_{ee}+\nu_{ei}
. Calculating this addition according to (
185
) would be incorrect, and
one has to use (
182
) instead. Comparison of Braginskii viscosities and heat conductivities with the BGK operator can
be found in Appendix
E.3
.

VIII.3
Fluid hierarchy

Even though we do not calculate the collisional integrals for general n-th order moments, we find it useful to discuss the fluid hierarchy and
formulate it for a general collisional operator
C(f_{a})
. One defines heat flux vectors, stress-tensors and fully
contracted moments according to

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2n+1)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{a}\int\boldsymbol{c}_{a}|\boldsymbol{c}_{a}|^{2n}f_{a}d^{3}v;

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2n)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{a}\int\big(\boldsymbol{c}_{a}\boldsymbol{c}_{a}-\frac{\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}}{3}|\boldsymbol{c}_{a}|^{2}\big)|\boldsymbol{c}_{a}|^{2n-2}f_{a}d^{3}v;

\displaystyle X^{(2n)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{a}\int|\boldsymbol{c}_{a}|^{2n}f_{a}d^{3}v=(2n+1)!!\frac{p^{n}_{a}}{\rho^{n-1}_{a}}+\widetilde{X}^{(2n)}_{a},

(187)

together with collisional contributions

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}}^{(2n+1)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{a}\int|\boldsymbol{c}_{a}|^{2n}\boldsymbol{c}_{a}C(f_{a})d^{3}v;

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}}}^{(2n)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{a}\int|\boldsymbol{c}_{a}|^{2n-2}\boldsymbol{c}_{a}\boldsymbol{c}_{a}C(f_{a})d^{3}v;

\displaystyle Q^{(2n)}_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{a}\int|\boldsymbol{c}_{a}|^{2n}C(f_{a})d^{3}v;\qquad Q_{a}=\frac{m_{a}}{2}\int|\boldsymbol{c}_{a}|^{2}C(f_{a})d^{3}v,

(188)

where to prevent incompatibility with the previous notation, for vectors and matrices
we use
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}
(mathcal Q) instead of
\boldsymbol{Q}
. The new notation fixes the problem that
for example
\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(3)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}
was used for the right hand side of evolution equation of the heat flux
\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}
, and not for
\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(3)}_{a}
.
It also clarifies that in the vector notation the matrix
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}}}^{(2n)}_{a}=\textrm{Tr}\textrm{Tr}\ldots\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}_{a}^{(2n)}
.
Note that
Q^{(2)}_{a}=2Q_{a}
;
X^{(2)}_{a}=3p_{a}
;
\widetilde{X}^{(2)}_{a}=0
;
\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(3)}_{a}=2\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}
and
\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(1)}_{a}=0
.

Fully non-linear evolution equations are given in Appendix
D
; see (
D13
)-(
D15
).
In the semi-linear approximation, these simplify into evolution equations for vectors valid for
n\geq 1

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2n+1)}_{a}+\frac{1}{3}\nabla\widetilde{X}^{(2n+2)}_{a}+\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2n+2)}_{a}-\frac{(2n+3)!!}{3}\frac{p_{a}^{n}}{\rho_{a}^{n}}\nabla\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a}+\Omega_{a}\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2n+1)}_{a}

\displaystyle+(2n+3)!!\frac{(n)}{3}\frac{p_{a}^{n}}{\rho_{a}^{n-1}}\nabla\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)=\vec{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}}^{(2n+1)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}=\vec{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}}^{(2n+1)}_{a}-\frac{(2n+3)!!}{3}\frac{p_{a}^{n}}{\rho_{a}^{n}}\boldsymbol{R}_{a},

(189)

stress-tensors valid for
n\geq 1

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2n)}_{a}+\frac{1}{5}\Big[\big(\nabla\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2n+1)}_{a}\big)^{S}-\frac{2}{3}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2n+1)}_{a}\Big]+\Omega_{a}\big(\boldsymbol{\hat{b}}\times\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2n)}_{a}\big)^{S}

\displaystyle+\frac{(2n+3)!!}{15}\frac{p^{n}_{a}}{\rho_{a}^{n-1}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{W}}}_{a}=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}}}^{(2n)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}}}^{(2n)}_{a}-\frac{\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}}{3}Q^{(2n)}_{a},

(190)

and scalar perturbations valid for
n\geq 2

\displaystyle\frac{d_{a}}{dt}\widetilde{X}^{(2n)}_{a}+\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(2n+1)}_{a}-(2n+1)!!\frac{(2n)}{3}\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)^{n-1}\nabla\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{Q}^{(2n)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}=Q^{(2n)}_{a}-(2n+1)!!\frac{(2n)}{3}\Big(\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big)^{n-1}Q_{a},

(191)

where (n) without species index should not be confused with the number density.
Equation (
191
) is also valid for
n=1
, but it is identically zero.
In comparison to previous notation

\vec{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}}^{(3)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}=2\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(3)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}
;
\vec{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}}^{(5)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}=\vec{\boldsymbol{Q}}^{(5)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}
;

\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}}}^{(2)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(2)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}
; and
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}}}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{Q}}}^{(4)}_{a}\,{}^{\prime}
.

VIII.4
Reducible & irreducible Hermite polynomials

The
irreducible
Hermite polynomials
H(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})
(notation without tilde) are usually defined through Laguerre-Sonine polynomials

L(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})
(see for example equation (G1.4.4), page 326 of
Balescu 1988
)

\displaystyle H^{(2n)}(\tilde{c})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L_{n}^{(1/2)}(\frac{\tilde{c}^{2}}{2});\qquad H^{(2n+1)}_{i}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})=\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}\tilde{c}_{i}L_{n}^{(3/2)}(\frac{\tilde{c}^{2}}{2});

\displaystyle H^{(2n)}_{ij}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{15}{8}}(\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\frac{\tilde{c}^{2}}{3}\delta_{ij})L_{n-1}^{(5/2)}(\frac{\tilde{c}^{2}}{2}),

(192)

where we use tilde for the normalized fluctuating velocity
\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}=\sqrt{m_{a}/T_{a}}\boldsymbol{c}_{a}
with species indices dropped.
In our calculations we find it more natural to use the
reducible
Hermite polynomials
\tilde{H}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})
(notation with tilde) of Grad
defined according to

\tilde{H}^{(m)}_{r_{1}r_{2}\ldots r_{m}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})=(-1)^{m}e^{\frac{\tilde{c}^{2}}{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{c}_{r_{1}}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{c}_{r_{2}}}\cdots\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{c}_{r_{m}}}e^{-\frac{\tilde{c}^{2}}{2}}.

(193)

By applying a sufficient number of contractions then yields definitions of fully contracted scalars, vectors and matrices

\tilde{H}^{(2n)}=\tilde{H}^{(2n)}_{r_{1}r_{1}\ldots r_{n}r_{n}};\qquad\tilde{H}^{(2n+1)}_{i}=\tilde{H}^{(2n+1)}_{ir_{1}r_{1}\ldots r_{n}r_{n}};\qquad\tilde{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}=\tilde{H}^{(2n)}_{ijr_{1}r_{1}\ldots r_{n-1}r_{n-1}},

(194)

together with conveniently defined traceless matrices (notation with hat)

\hat{H}_{ij}^{(2n)}=\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(2n)}-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(2n)}.

(195)

The relation between irreducible and reducible Hermite polynomials then can be shown to be

\displaystyle H^{(2n)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{2^{n}n!(2n+1)!!}\Big)^{1/2}\tilde{H}^{(2n)};\qquad H^{(2n+1)}_{i}=\Big(\frac{3}{2^{n}n!(2n+3)!!}\Big)^{1/2}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(2n+1)};

\displaystyle H^{(2n)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{15}{2^{n}(n-1)!(2n+3)!!}\Big)^{1/2}\hat{H}_{ij}^{(2n)},

(196)

and both approaches use essentially the same polynomials, the only difference is the location of normalization factors.
The reducible Hermite polynomials are used to define Hermite moments

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(2n)}=\frac{1}{n_{a}}\int f_{a}\tilde{H}^{(2n)}d^{3}c;\qquad\tilde{h}^{(2n+1)}_{i}=\frac{1}{n_{a}}\int f_{a}\tilde{H}^{(2n+1)}_{i}d^{3}c;\qquad\hat{h}^{(2n)}_{ij}=\frac{1}{n_{a}}\int f_{a}\hat{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}d^{3}c,

(197)

and analogously for the irreducible ones. Note that the scalar
\tilde{h}^{(2)}=0
,
and we thus often use
\hat{h}^{(2)}_{ij}=\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{ij}=(1/n_{a})\int f_{a}\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{ij}d^{3}c
. Finally, by using orthogonality relations one obtains
perturbation
\chi_{a}
of the distribution function
f_{a}=f_{a}^{(0)}(1+\chi_{a})
around Maxwellian
f_{a}^{(0)}
, in the following form

\displaystyle\chi_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}\Big[\frac{15}{2^{n}(n-1)!(2n+3)!!}\hat{h}^{(2n)}_{ij}\hat{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}+\frac{1}{2^{n}n!(2n+1)!!}\tilde{h}^{(2n)}\tilde{H}^{(2n)}

(198)

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2^{n}n!(2n+3)!!}\tilde{h}^{(2n+1)}_{i}\tilde{H}^{(2n+1)}_{i}\Big];

\displaystyle\chi_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}\Big[{h}^{(2n)}_{ij}{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}+{h}^{(2n)}{H}^{(2n)}+{h}^{(2n+1)}_{i}{H}^{(2n+1)}_{i}\Big],

(199)

and the two approaches are equivalent. Alternatively, because
\hat{h}^{(2n)}_{ij}
are traceless,
it is possible to use
\hat{h}^{(2n)}_{ij}\hat{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}=\hat{h}^{(2n)}_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}
. Note that
2^{n}n!(2n+1)!!=(2n+1)!
.
The 13-moment model of Burgers-Schunk is obtained by
N=1
. Prescribing
N=2
yields the 22-moment model

\chi_{a}=\frac{1}{2}\hat{h}_{ij}^{(2)}\hat{H}_{ij}^{(2)}+\frac{1}{10}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(3)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(3)}+\frac{1}{28}\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}\hat{H}_{ij}^{(4)}+\frac{1}{120}\tilde{h}^{(4)}\tilde{H}^{(4)}+\frac{1}{280}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(5)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(5)},

(200)

with Hermite polynomials

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{i}(\tilde{c}^{2}-5);\qquad\tilde{H}^{(5)}_{i}=\tilde{c}_{i}(\tilde{c}^{4}-14\tilde{c}^{2}+35);

\displaystyle\hat{H}^{(2)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{c}^{2}\big);\qquad\hat{H}^{(4)}_{ij}=\big(\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{c}^{2}\big)(\tilde{c}^{2}-7);\qquad\tilde{H}^{(4)}=\tilde{c}^{4}-10\tilde{c}^{2}+15,

(201)

and neglecting
\tilde{h}^{(4)}=0
(meaning
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}=0
) yields the 21-moment model.

Transformation from Hermite to fluid moments is done according to

\displaystyle\tilde{\boldsymbol{h}}^{(3)}_{a}=\frac{2}{p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a};\qquad\tilde{\boldsymbol{h}}^{(5)}_{a}=\frac{1}{p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}\Big(\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}-28\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\Big);

\displaystyle\hat{\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{h}}}}^{(2)}_{a}=\tilde{\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{h}}}}^{(2)}_{a}=\frac{1}{p_{a}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a};\qquad\hat{\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{h}}}}^{(4)}_{a}=\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}^{2}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}-\frac{7}{p_{a}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}_{a};\qquad\tilde{h}^{(4)}_{a}=\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}^{2}}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}_{a}.

(202)

Various models are summarized
in Tables
VIII.1
&
VIII.2
. In Table
VIII.1
the perturbation
\chi_{a}
is given in reducible Hermite moments,
and in Table
VIII.2
the perturbation is given in fluid moments.

Model name

Corresponding perturbation of
f_{a}=f_{a}^{(0)}(1+\chi_{a})
in Hermite moments

;

\chi_{a}=\frac{1}{10}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(3)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(3)};

\chi_{a}=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(2)}\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(2)};

\chi_{a}=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(2)}\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(2)}+\frac{1}{10}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(3)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(3)};

\chi_{a}=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(2)}\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(2)}+\frac{1}{6}\tilde{h}_{ijk}^{(3)}\tilde{H}_{ijk}^{(3)};
