where
\chi_{a}
represents the wanted perturbation. One can go quickly between physical and normalized units by

\int f_{a}(\boldsymbol{c})d^{3}c=n_{a}\int\phi^{(0)}(1+\chi_{a}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}))d^{3}\tilde{c}.

(B8)

The tensorial polynomials are again orthogonal to each other, where by using “weight”

\phi^{(0)}

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1;

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(1)}_{i}\tilde{H}^{(1)}_{j}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{ij};

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{kl}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk};

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{3}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{2}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{3}}

(B9)

\displaystyle+\delta_{r_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{2}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}},

and expressions quickly become long

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}r_{4}}\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}s_{4}}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{4}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{3}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{4}}

(B10)

\displaystyle+\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{2}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{3}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{2}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{r_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{4}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{3}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{4}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{r_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{1}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{3}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{1}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{r_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{4}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{2}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{4}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{r_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{1}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{2}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{1}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{r_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{3}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{2}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{3}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{r_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{1}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{2}}+\delta_{r_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{4}s_{1}}.

The general orthogonality can be written by introducing multi-indices
\boldsymbol{r}=r_{1}\ldots r_{n}
and
\boldsymbol{s}=s_{1}\dots s_{m}

\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(n)}_{\boldsymbol{r}}\tilde{H}^{(m)}_{\boldsymbol{s}}d^{3}\tilde{c}=\delta_{mn}\boldsymbol{\delta}^{(n)}_{\boldsymbol{r}\boldsymbol{s}},

(B11)

where the new symbol
\boldsymbol{\delta}^{(n)}_{\boldsymbol{r}\boldsymbol{s}}
is equal to one if indices
r_{1}\ldots r_{n}
are a permutation
of
s_{1}\ldots s_{n}
and otherwise it is zero. In other words, for
n=m
the right hand side contains
n!
terms, where each of these terms
has a form
\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\ldots\delta_{r_{n}s_{n}}
and to calculate the other terms keep
r
-indices fixed, and do all the
possible permutations with
s
-indices (or vice versa).
A particular case of (
B11
) reads

m\neq 0:\qquad\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(m)}_{\boldsymbol{s}}d^{3}\tilde{c}=0,

(B12)

i.e. integral over a single Hermite polynomial with weight
\phi^{(0)}
is zero.

The goal of the Hermite expansion is to find perturbation of the
distribution function
\chi_{a}
in (
B7
). For the most general decomposition,
one can chose to express the perturbation
\chi_{a}
as a sum of Hermite polynomials

\displaystyle\chi_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}A_{r_{1}r_{2}\ldots r_{m}}^{(m)}\tilde{H}_{r_{1}r_{2}\ldots r_{m}}^{(m)}

(B13)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{r_{1}}^{(1)}\tilde{H}_{r_{1}}^{(1)}+A_{r_{1}r_{2}}^{(2)}\tilde{H}_{r_{1}r_{2}}^{(2)}+A_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}^{(3)}\tilde{H}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}^{(3)}+A_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}r_{4}}^{(4)}\tilde{H}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}r_{4}}^{(4)}+\cdots,

where coefficients
A_{r_{1}r_{2}\ldots r_{m}}^{(m)}
need to be found. Note that full contractions over all indices are present and the result is a scalar.
Multiplying (
B13
) by weight
\phi^{(0)}
and polynomial
\tilde{H}_{s_{1}s_{2}\ldots s_{n}}^{(n)}
and integrating over
d^{3}\tilde{c}
by using
orthogonality (
B11
) then yields

\displaystyle\int\chi_{a}\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}_{\boldsymbol{s}}^{(n)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=A_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{(n)}\delta^{(n)}_{\boldsymbol{r}\boldsymbol{s}}=n!A_{\boldsymbol{s}}^{(n)},

(B14)

where the last equality holds because coefficient
A_{\boldsymbol{s}}^{(n)}
is a fluid variable and symmetric in all of its indices. Coefficients

A_{\boldsymbol{s}}^{(n)}
are thus found according to

\displaystyle A_{\boldsymbol{s}}^{(n)}=\frac{1}{n!}\int\chi_{a}\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}_{\boldsymbol{s}}^{(n)}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})d^{3}\tilde{c}=\frac{1}{n!}\int(1+\chi_{a})\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}_{\boldsymbol{s}}^{(n)}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})d^{3}\tilde{c}=\frac{1}{n!}\Big[\underbrace{\frac{1}{n_{a}}\int f_{a}\tilde{H}_{\boldsymbol{s}}^{(n)}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})d^{3}c}_{\tilde{h}^{(n)}_{\boldsymbol{s}}}\Big],

(B15)

where we have used orthogonality relation (
B12
) and changed the integration variable to
d^{3}c
with (
B8
).
Quantities in the brackets of (
B15
) are called
Hermite moments

\tilde{h}^{(n)}_{\boldsymbol{s}}
.
The entire Hermite expansion then can be summarized
into two easy steps:

1) Calculate Hermite moments

\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{2}\ldots r_{m}}^{a(m)}=\frac{1}{n_{a}}\int f_{a}\tilde{H}_{r_{1}r_{2}\ldots r_{m}}^{a(m)}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})d^{3}c,

(B16)

2) the final perturbation is

\chi_{a}=\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m!}\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{2}\ldots r_{m}}^{a(m)}\tilde{H}_{r_{1}r_{2}\ldots r_{m}}^{a(m)}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}).

(B17)

It is useful to omit writing the species indices “a” on both
\tilde{h}
and
\tilde{H}
, as well as on fluid moments,
we will keep the species index only for
n_{a},m_{a},T_{a},p_{a}
. The final perturbations will be written in a full form.

By using definitions of general fluid moments, one straightforwardly calculates Hermite moments

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(1)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a}}\int f_{a}\tilde{H}^{(1)}_{i}d^{3}c=0;

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a}}\int f_{a}\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{ij}d^{3}c=\frac{1}{p_{a}}\Pi_{ij}^{(2)};

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{ijk}^{(3)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a}}\int f_{a}\tilde{H}_{ijk}^{(3)}d^{3}c=\frac{1}{p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}q_{ijk},

(B18)

together with

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{ijkl}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a}}\int f_{a}\tilde{H}_{ijkl}^{(4)}(\boldsymbol{c})d^{3}c=\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}^{2}}r_{ijkl}+\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk}

(B19)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{p_{a}}\big(\delta_{ij}p_{kl}+\delta_{jk}p_{li}+\delta_{kl}p_{ij}+\delta_{li}p_{jk}+\delta_{ik}p_{jl}+\delta_{jl}p_{ik}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}^{2}}r_{ijkl}-\big(\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{p_{a}}\big(\delta_{ij}\Pi_{kl}^{(2)}+\delta_{jk}\Pi_{li}^{(2)}+\delta_{kl}\Pi_{ij}^{(2)}+\delta_{li}\Pi_{jk}^{(2)}+\delta_{ik}\Pi_{jl}^{(2)}+\delta_{jl}\Pi_{ik}^{(2)}\big),

and

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{ijklm}^{(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{a}}\int f_{a}\tilde{H}_{ijklm}^{(5)}(\boldsymbol{c})d^{3}c=\frac{\rho_{a}^{3/2}}{p_{a}^{5/2}}X^{(5)}_{ijklm}-\frac{\rho_{a}^{1/2}}{p_{a}^{3/2}}\Big(\delta_{ij}q_{klm}+\delta_{jk}q_{lim}+\delta_{kl}q_{ijm}+\delta_{li}q_{jkm}

(B20)

\displaystyle+\delta_{ik}q_{jlm}+\delta_{jl}q_{ikm}+\delta_{im}q_{jkl}+\delta_{jm}q_{ikl}+\delta_{km}q_{ijl}+\delta_{lm}q_{ijk}\Big).

B.1
Usual perturbations of Grad

B.1.1
20-moment model

By using the definition of the perturbation (
B17
) and cutting the hierarchy at

\chi_{a}=\tilde{h}_{i}^{(1)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(1)}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(2)}\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(2)}+\frac{1}{6}\tilde{h}_{ijk}^{(3)}\tilde{H}_{ijk}^{(3)},

(B21)

yields the 20-moment perturbation of Grad

\textrm{20-moment}:\qquad\chi_{a}=\frac{m_{a}}{2p_{a}T_{a}}\big(\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}:\boldsymbol{c}_{a}\boldsymbol{c}_{a}\big)+\frac{m_{a}^{2}}{6p_{a}T_{a}^{2}}\big(\boldsymbol{c}_{a}\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{a}:\boldsymbol{c}_{a}\boldsymbol{c}_{a}\big)-\frac{m_{a}}{p_{a}T_{a}}\big(\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\cdot\boldsymbol{c}_{a}\big),

(B22)

where one defines vector
\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}=(1/2)\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{a}
.

B.1.2
13-moment model

To quickly obtain the simplified 13-moment model of Grad, one can use

\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{q}}}=(2/5)(\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}})^{S}+\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}
with
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}
neglected (validity of this equation is shown below) and calculating

\boldsymbol{c}\cdot\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{q}}}:\boldsymbol{c}\boldsymbol{c}=(6/5)(\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}\cdot\boldsymbol{c})c^{2}
yields the 13-moment model

\textrm{13-moment}:\qquad\chi_{a}=\frac{m_{a}}{2p_{a}T_{a}}\big(\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}:\boldsymbol{c}_{a}\boldsymbol{c}_{a}\big)-\frac{m_{a}}{p_{a}T_{a}}\big(\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\cdot\boldsymbol{c}_{a}\big)\Big(1-\frac{m_{a}}{5T_{a}}c_{a}^{2}\Big).

(B23)

To re-derive the heat flux contribution in the 13-moment model from scratch can be done by using a contracted Hermite polynomial

\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{i}\equiv\delta_{jk}\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{ijk}=\tilde{c}_{i}(\tilde{c}^{2}-5).

(B24)

However, one needs to be careful about the normalization constant because applying
contractions
\delta_{r_{1}r_{2}}
and
\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}
on (
B9
) yields

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{i}\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{j}d^{3}\tilde{c}=10\delta_{ij},

(B25)

which can be also verified by direct calculation. (Note that it is important to apply contractions
on (
B9
) as stated
above, and not accidentally as
\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}
which would yield an erroneous coefficient 20, the contraction
must satisfy definition (
B24
)). Then one calculates Hermite moment

\tilde{h}_{i}^{(3)}=\frac{1}{n_{a}}\int f_{a}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(3)}d^{3}c=\frac{2}{p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}\vec{q}_{i},

(B26)

(which is equal to
\tilde{h}_{ikk}^{(3)}
) and the heat flux perturbation becomes

\textrm{8-moment}:\qquad\chi_{a}=\frac{1}{10}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(3)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(3)}=-\,\frac{m_{a}}{p_{a}T_{a}}\big(\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\cdot\boldsymbol{c}_{a}\big)\Big(1-\frac{m_{a}}{5T_{a}}c_{a}^{2}\Big),

(B27)

recovering (
B23
).

B.1.3
Double-checking the fluid moments

By using the 8-moment perturbation (
B27
) (or the perturbation of the 13-moment model (
B23
)),
it is possible to calculate the heat flux moment,
for example by switching to normalized units
and using integral (
B89
) valid for any vector
\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}
, yielding

\displaystyle\textrm{13-moment}:\qquad q_{ijk}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{a}\int c_{i}c_{j}c_{k}f_{a}^{(0)}(1+\chi_{a})d^{3}c=-\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}(\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}\cdot\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})\big(1-\frac{\tilde{c}^{2}}{5}\big)\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}

(B28)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{5}\big[\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}\big]^{S}_{ijk}.

In contrast, by using the 20-moment perturbation (
B22
) and integral (
B91
) yields identity
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{q}}}=\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{q}}}
, as it should be. Thus,
the full heat flux tensor can be decomposed as

\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{q}}}=\frac{2}{5}\big[\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}\big]^{S}+\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime},

(B29)

where
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}
represents the highest-order irreducible part of the heat flux tensor,
and by applying trace at (
B29
) it can be verified that
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}
is traceless.
Calculation of the 4th-order moment
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{r}}}
yields (with either 10, 13 or 20-moment model)

\displaystyle r_{ijkl}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{a}\int c_{i}c_{j}c_{k}c_{l}f_{a}^{(0)}(1+\chi_{a})d^{3}c

(B30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}\tilde{c}_{l}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}+\frac{p_{a}}{2\rho_{a}}\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}\tilde{c}_{l}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}:\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\Big[\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{jk}\delta_{il}\Big]+\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big[\delta_{ij}\Pi_{kl}^{(2)}+\delta_{ik}\Pi_{jl}^{(2)}+\delta_{il}\Pi_{jk}^{(2)}+\delta_{jk}\Pi_{il}^{(2)}+\delta_{jl}\Pi_{ik}^{(2)}+\delta_{kl}\Pi_{ij}^{(2)}\Big],

where one can use integrals (
B84
), (
B100
). Applying trace at (
B30
) yields

\displaystyle\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{r}}}=5\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}+7\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(2)};\qquad\textrm{Tr}\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{r}}}=15\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}.

(B31)

If one does not want to use our provided integrals from Appendix
B.6
(or wants to verify them),
all the needed integrals can be calculated by using the powerfull orthogonality theorem. As an example

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{lmn}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=\int\tilde{H}_{ijk}^{(3)}\tilde{H}_{lmn}^{(3)}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}+\int\big(\delta_{ij}\tilde{c}_{k}+\delta_{jk}\tilde{c}_{i}+\delta_{ik}\tilde{c}_{j}\big)\tilde{H}_{lmn}^{(3)}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c},

(B32)

where the first term is calculated with orthogonality (
B9
), and the second term is zero
(because all the resulting terms can be re-written as
\tilde{H}_{i}^{(1)}\tilde{H}_{lmn}^{(3)}
which yields zero after integration;
see also integral (
B83
)).

In some calculations, one actually does not need to work with the complicated right hand side of (
B9
), because
once the integral is calculated, the result is going to be applied on
\tilde{h}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}^{(3)}
, which is a fluid variable and symmetric in all of
its indices. Let us demonstrate it by using the 20-moment heat flux perturbation

\displaystyle\chi_{a}=\frac{1}{6}\tilde{h}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}^{(3)}\tilde{H}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}^{(3)}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}),

(B33)

and calculate the heat flux moment again, this time with the Hermite variables

\displaystyle q_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{a}\int c_{r_{1}}c_{r_{2}}c_{r_{3}}f_{a}^{(0)}(1+\chi_{a})d^{3}c

(B34)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{6}\sqrt{\frac{T_{a}}{m_{a}}}\tilde{h}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}^{(3)}\int\tilde{c}_{r_{1}}\tilde{c}_{r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{r_{3}}\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{6}\sqrt{\frac{T_{a}}{m_{a}}}\tilde{h}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}^{(3)}\int\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{6}\sqrt{\frac{T_{a}}{m_{a}}}\tilde{h}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}^{(3)}\boldsymbol{\delta}^{(3)}_{(r_{1}r_{2}r_{3})(s_{1}s_{2}s_{3})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{a}\sqrt{\frac{T_{a}}{m_{a}}}\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}^{(3)}.

In the derivation, we did not use the complicated right hand side of (
B9
), we only used

\tilde{h}_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{(n)}\boldsymbol{\delta}^{(n)}_{\boldsymbol{rs}}=n!\tilde{h}_{\boldsymbol{s}}^{(n)}
, and the factor of
3!
cancelled out as well.

Similarly, using the same perturbation (
B33
) one can derive the 5th-order fluid moment
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(5)}
,
by using the Hermite polynomial
\tilde{H}^{(5)}_{ijklm}
, equation (
B5
), according to

\displaystyle X^{(5)}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}r_{4}r_{5}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{a}\int c_{r_{1}}c_{r_{2}}c_{r_{3}}c_{r_{4}}c_{r_{5}}f_{a}^{(0)}(1+\chi_{a})d^{3}c

(B35)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{6}\Big(\frac{T_{a}}{m_{a}}\Big)^{3/2}\tilde{h}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}^{(3)}\int\tilde{c}_{r_{1}}\tilde{c}_{r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{r_{3}}\tilde{c}_{r_{4}}\tilde{c}_{r_{5}}\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{6}\Big(\frac{T_{a}}{m_{a}}\Big)^{3/2}\tilde{h}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}^{(3)}\int\Big[\delta_{r_{1}r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{r_{3}}\tilde{c}_{r_{4}}\tilde{c}_{r_{5}}+\delta_{r_{2}r_{3}}\tilde{c}_{r_{4}}\tilde{c}_{r_{1}}\tilde{c}_{r_{5}}+\delta_{r_{3}r_{4}}\tilde{c}_{r_{1}}\tilde{c}_{r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{r_{5}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{4}r_{1}}\tilde{c}_{r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{r_{3}}\tilde{c}_{r_{5}}+\delta_{r_{1}r_{3}}\tilde{c}_{r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{r_{4}}\tilde{c}_{r_{5}}+\delta_{r_{2}r_{4}}\tilde{c}_{r_{1}}\tilde{c}_{r_{3}}\tilde{c}_{r_{5}}+\delta_{r_{1}r_{5}}\tilde{c}_{r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{r_{3}}\tilde{c}_{r_{4}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{2}r_{5}}\tilde{c}_{r_{1}}\tilde{c}_{r_{3}}\tilde{c}_{r_{4}}+\delta_{r_{3}r_{5}}\tilde{c}_{r_{1}}\tilde{c}_{r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{r_{4}}+\delta_{r_{4}r_{5}}\tilde{c}_{r_{1}}\tilde{c}_{r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{r_{3}}\Big]\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{a}\Big(\frac{T_{a}}{m_{a}}\Big)^{3/2}\Big[\delta_{r_{1}r_{2}}\tilde{h}_{r_{3}r_{4}r_{5}}^{(3)}+\delta_{r_{2}r_{3}}\tilde{h}_{r_{4}r_{1}r_{5}}^{(3)}+\delta_{r_{3}r_{4}}\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{5}}^{(3)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{4}r_{1}}\tilde{h}_{r_{2}r_{3}r_{5}}^{(3)}+\delta_{r_{1}r_{3}}\tilde{h}_{r_{2}r_{4}r_{5}}^{(3)}+\delta_{r_{2}r_{4}}\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{3}r_{5}}^{(3)}+\delta_{r_{1}r_{5}}\tilde{h}_{r_{2}r_{3}r_{4}}^{(3)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{2}r_{5}}\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{3}r_{4}}^{(3)}+\delta_{r_{3}r_{5}}\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{4}}^{(3)}+\delta_{r_{4}r_{5}}\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}^{(3)}\Big].

Or rewriten with the heat fluxes according to (
B18
) and using usual indices

\displaystyle X^{(5)}_{ijklm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big[\delta_{ij}q_{klm}+\delta_{jk}q_{lim}+\delta_{kl}q_{ijm}+\delta_{li}q_{jkm}+\delta_{ik}q_{jlm}

(B36)

\displaystyle+\delta_{jl}q_{ikm}+\delta_{im}q_{jkl}+\delta_{jm}q_{ikl}+\delta_{km}q_{ijl}+\delta_{lm}q_{ijk}\Big],

and by using heat flux decomposition (
B29
) with
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\prime}
neglected

\displaystyle X^{(5)}_{ijklm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{5}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big[q_{i}\big(\delta_{jk}\delta_{lm}+\delta_{kl}\delta_{jm}+\delta_{jl}\delta_{km}\big)+q_{j}\big(\delta_{kl}\delta_{im}+\delta_{il}\delta_{km}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{lm}\big)

(B37)

\displaystyle+q_{k}\big(\delta_{ij}\delta_{lm}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jm}+\delta_{jl}\delta_{im}\big)+q_{l}\big(\delta_{ij}\delta_{km}+\delta_{jk}\delta_{im}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jm}\big)

\displaystyle+q_{m}\big(\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}+\delta_{jk}\delta_{il}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}\big)\Big].

Applying contractions at (
B36
) yields

\displaystyle\big[\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(5)}\big]_{ijk}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\Big[2\big(\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}\big)^{S}+9\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{q}}}\Big]_{ijk}=\frac{28}{5}\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\big(\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}\big)^{S}_{ijk}+9\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\sigma_{ijk}\,^{\prime};

\displaystyle\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textrm{Tr}\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(5)}=28\frac{p_{a}}{\rho_{a}}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}.

(B38)

B.2
Higher-order perturbations (full
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(4)}
and
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(5)}
moments)

By using the technique described above, it is possible to use the following higher-order perturbation

\chi_{a}=\frac{1}{2!}\tilde{h}_{s_{1}s_{2}}^{(2)}\tilde{H}_{s_{1}s_{2}}^{(2)}+\frac{1}{3!}\tilde{h}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}^{(3)}\tilde{H}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}^{(3)}+\frac{1}{4!}\tilde{h}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}s_{4}}^{(4)}\tilde{H}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}s_{4}}^{(4)}+\frac{1}{5!}\tilde{h}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}s_{4}s_{5}}^{(5)}\tilde{H}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}s_{4}s_{5}}^{(5)},

(B39)

and directly calculate fluid moments (we use
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(4)}
instead of
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{r}}}
from now on)

\displaystyle\big[\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(4)}\big]_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}r_{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\Big[\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}r_{4}}^{(4)}+\delta_{r_{1}r_{2}}\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{r_{3}r_{4}}+\delta_{r_{2}r_{3}}\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{r_{1}r_{4}}+\delta_{r_{3}r_{4}}\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{r_{1}r_{2}}

(B40)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{1}r_{4}}\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{r_{2}r_{3}}+\delta_{r_{1}r_{3}}\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{r_{2}r_{4}}+\delta_{r_{2}r_{4}}\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{r_{1}r_{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{1}r_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}r_{4}}+\delta_{r_{1}r_{3}}\delta_{r_{2}r_{4}}+\delta_{r_{2}r_{3}}\delta_{r_{1}r_{4}}\Big],

and

\displaystyle\big[\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{(5)}\big]_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}r_{4}r_{5}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p_{a}\Big(\frac{T_{a}}{m_{a}}\Big)^{3/2}\Big[\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}r_{4}r_{5}}^{(5)}+\delta_{r_{1}r_{2}}\tilde{h}_{r_{3}r_{4}r_{5}}^{(3)}+\delta_{r_{2}r_{3}}\tilde{h}_{r_{4}r_{1}r_{5}}^{(3)}+\delta_{r_{3}r_{4}}\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{5}}^{(3)}

(B41)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{4}r_{1}}\tilde{h}_{r_{2}r_{3}r_{5}}^{(3)}+\delta_{r_{1}r_{3}}\tilde{h}_{r_{2}r_{4}r_{5}}^{(3)}+\delta_{r_{2}r_{4}}\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{3}r_{5}}^{(3)}+\delta_{r_{1}r_{5}}\tilde{h}_{r_{2}r_{3}r_{4}}^{(3)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{2}r_{5}}\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{3}r_{4}}^{(3)}+\delta_{r_{3}r_{5}}\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{4}}^{(3)}+\delta_{r_{4}r_{5}}\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}^{(3)}\Big].

Both results contain new contributions, represented by the
\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}r_{4}}^{(4)}
and
\tilde{h}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}r_{4}r_{5}}^{(5)}
.

It is useful to introduce notation where by applying contraction at a tensor, the contracted indices will be suppressed,
so for example
\tilde{h}_{i}^{(3)}\equiv\tilde{h}_{ikk}^{(3)}
, or
X^{(4)}_{ij}\equiv X^{(4)}_{ijkk}
and
X^{(4)}\equiv X^{(4)}_{iikk}
.
We define all the contractions without any additional factors, with the sole exception of the heat flux vector
\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}
where
the additional factor of
1/2
is present, to match its usual definition.
To emphasize this
difference, in the index notation we thus keep an arrow on the components of the heat flux vector
\vec{q}_{i}
, to clearly distinguish it from the
contracted tensor
q_{ijk}
.

By applying contractions at (
B40
), (
B41
) then yields

\displaystyle X^{(4)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\big[\tilde{h}^{(4)}_{ij}+7\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{ij}+5\delta_{ij}\big];

\displaystyle X^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\big[\tilde{h}^{(4)}+15\big];

\displaystyle X^{(5)}_{ijk}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{T_{a}}{m_{a}}}\Big[\tilde{h}^{(5)}_{ijk}+\delta_{ij}\tilde{h}^{(3)}_{k}+\delta_{jk}\tilde{h}^{(3)}_{i}+\delta_{ik}\tilde{h}^{(3)}_{j}+9\tilde{h}^{(3)}_{ijk}\Big];

\displaystyle X^{(5)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{T_{a}}{m_{a}}}\Big[\tilde{h}^{(5)}_{i}+14\tilde{h}^{(3)}_{i}\Big],

(B42)

and applying contractions at the Hermite moments (
B18
)-(
B20
) yields

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(2)}=\frac{1}{p_{a}}\Pi_{ij}^{(2)};\qquad\tilde{h}_{i}^{(3)}=\frac{2}{p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}\vec{q}_{i};

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(4)}=\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}^{2}}X_{ij}^{(4)}-5\delta_{ij}-\frac{7}{p_{a}}\Pi_{ij}^{(2)};

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(4)}=\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}^{2}}X^{(4)}-15;

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{ijk}^{(5)}=\frac{1}{p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}\Big[\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}X_{ijk}^{(5)}-\big(2\delta_{ij}\vec{q}_{k}+2\delta_{jk}\vec{q}_{i}+2\delta_{ik}\vec{q}_{j}+9q_{ijk}\big)\Big];

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{i}^{(5)}=\frac{1}{p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}\Big(\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}X^{(5)}_{i}-28\vec{q}_{i}\Big).

(B43)

B.2.1
Viscosity
\Pi^{(4)}_{ij}
of the 4th-order moment
X_{ij}^{(4)}

The usual visocity tensor is defined as a traceless matrix

\Pi_{ij}^{(2)}=m_{a}\int\big(c_{i}c_{j}-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{ij}c^{2}\big)f_{a}d^{3}c.

(B44)

Similarly, it is beneficial to introduce a traceless viscosity tensor of the 4th-order fluid moment

\Pi_{ij}^{(4)}=m_{a}\int\big(c_{i}c_{j}-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{ij}c^{2}\big)c^{2}f_{a}d^{3}c.

(B45)

In another words, the moment
X_{ij}^{(4)}
is decomposed as

X_{ij}^{(4)}=\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}X^{(4)}+\Pi_{ij}^{(4)},

(B46)

where the fully contracted
X^{(4)}=m_{a}\int c^{4}f_{a}d^{3}c
. Scalar
X^{(4)}
is further decomposed to its “core” Maxwellian part, and additional
perturbation
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}
(with wide tilde) according to

X^{(4)}=15\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}+\widetilde{X}^{(4)},

(B47)

and the corresponding Hermite moments thus become

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}^{2}}\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}+\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}^{2}}\Pi_{ij}^{(4)}-\frac{7}{p_{a}}\Pi_{ij}^{(2)};

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}^{2}}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}.

(B48)

It is important to emphasize that depending on the choice of perturbation
\chi_{a}
, in general
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}
is non-zero.
However, this perturbation is not required to derive the model of
Braginskii 1965
, and for example

Balescu 1988
prescribes irreducible
h^{(4)}=0
. In the next section we will consider simplified perturbations and derive the
above results in a more direct manner, nevertheless, the more general case (
B39
) is a very useful guide that it is
possible to consider perturbations with non-zero
h^{(4)}
.

Finally, because the reducible matrix
\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(4)}
is not traceless in general (unless one prescribes Hermite closure

\tilde{h}^{(4)}=0
which makes it traceless by definition), it is useful to introduce traceless

\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}=\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(4)}-\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{h}^{(4)}=\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}^{2}}\Pi_{ij}^{(4)}-\frac{7}{p_{a}}\Pi_{ij}^{(2)},

(B49)

where we used hat instead of tilde.

B.2.2
Simplified perturbations (21-moment model)

Instead of working with very complicated perturbations (
B39
), it was shown by
Balescu 1988
that to obtain
the model of
Braginskii 1965
, it is suffucient to work with simplified

\chi_{a}=h_{ij}^{(2)}H_{ij}^{(2)}+h_{i}^{(3)}H_{i}^{(3)}+h_{ij}^{(4)}H_{ij}^{(4)}+h_{i}^{(5)}H_{i}^{(5)}.

(B50)

Perturbation (
B50
) is written with
irreducible
Hermite polynomials (notation without tilde), discussed in the next section.
This perturbation represents 21-moment model, and recovers both the stress-tensor and the heat flux of Braginskii.
However, the connection between irreducible and reducible Hermite polynomials can be very blurry at first,
and we continue with
reducible
Hermite polynomials.

Applying contractions at the hierarchy of reducible polynomials (
B5
) yields

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{i}(\tilde{c}^{2}-5);\qquad\tilde{H}^{(5)}_{i}=\tilde{c}_{i}(\tilde{c}^{4}-14\tilde{c}^{2}+35);

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\delta_{ij};\qquad\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{ij}=\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}(\tilde{c}^{2}-7)-\delta_{ij}(\tilde{c}^{2}-5).

(B51)

By using these polynomials, the Hermite moments then calculate

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{i}^{(3)}=\frac{2}{p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}\vec{q}_{i};\qquad\tilde{h}_{i}^{(5)}=\frac{1}{p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}\Big(\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}X^{(5)}_{i}-28\vec{q}_{i}\Big);

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(2)}=\frac{1}{p_{a}}\Pi_{ij}^{(2)};\qquad\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}=\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}^{2}}\Pi_{ij}^{(4)}-\frac{7}{p_{a}}\Pi_{ij}^{(2)},

of course recovering previous results. The reducible Hermite polynomials satisfy following orthogonality relations

\displaystyle\int\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{i}\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{j}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=10\delta_{ij};\qquad\int\tilde{H}^{(5)}_{i}\tilde{H}^{(5)}_{j}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=280\delta_{ij};

\displaystyle\int\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{kl}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk};

\displaystyle\int\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{kl}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=14\big(\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk}\big)+4\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl},

(B52)

and because Hermite moments
\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{kl}
,
\hat{h}^{(4)}_{kl}
are symmetric and traceless

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{kl}\int\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{kl}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=2\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{ij};\qquad\hat{h}^{(4)}_{kl}\int\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{kl}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=28\hat{h}^{(4)}_{ij}.

(B53)

Thus, a perturbation which can be directly derived from the hierarchy of reducible Hermite polynomials
(with no reference to irreducible Hermite polynomials or Laguerre-Sonine polynomials) reads

\chi_{a}=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(2)}\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(2)}+\frac{1}{10}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(3)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(3)}+\frac{1}{28}\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(4)}+\frac{1}{280}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(5)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(5)},

(B54)

where each term is calculated as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{10}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(3)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(3)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{5p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}(\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\cdot\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a})(\tilde{c}_{a}^{2}-5);

\displaystyle\frac{1}{280}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(5)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{280p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}\Big[\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}(\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}\cdot\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a})-28(\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\cdot\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a})\Big](\tilde{c}_{a}^{4}-14\tilde{c}_{a}^{2}+35);

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(2)}\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2p_{a}}\big(\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}:\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a}\big);

\displaystyle\frac{1}{28}\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{28}\Big[\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}^{2}}\big(\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}:\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a}\big)-\frac{7}{p_{a}}\big(\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}:\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a}\big)\Big](\tilde{c}_{a}^{2}-7),

(B55)

with normalized velocity
\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a}=\sqrt{m_{a}/T_{a}}\boldsymbol{c}_{a}
. Bellow we show that perturbation (
B54
), (
B55
)
is equivalent to the perturbation of Balescu (
B50
)
obtained with irreducible polynomials.
The final perturbation of the 21-moment model which recovers
Braginskii 1965
thus reads

\displaystyle\chi_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2p_{a}}\big(\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}:\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a}\big)+\frac{1}{28}\Big[\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}^{2}}\big(\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}^{(4)}_{a}:\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a}\big)-\frac{7}{p_{a}}\big(\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}_{a}^{(2)}:\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a}\big)\Big](\tilde{c}_{a}^{2}-7)

(B56)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{5p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}(\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\cdot\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a})(\tilde{c}_{a}^{2}-5)+\frac{1}{280p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}\Big[\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}(\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}_{a}\cdot\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a})-28(\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}_{a}\cdot\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}_{a})\Big](\tilde{c}_{a}^{4}-14\tilde{c}_{a}^{2}+35).

Finally, because
\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}
is traceless, its double contraction with
\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(4)}
makes the part of this
polynomial proportional to
\delta_{ij}
redundant in the final perturbation. It is possible to define another traceless polynomial
(with hat instead of tilde)

\hat{H}_{ij}^{(4)}=\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(4)}-\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{H}^{(4)}=\big(\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{c}^{2}\big)(\tilde{c}^{2}-7),

(B57)

and replace the following term in the perturbation (
B54
)

\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(4)}=\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}\hat{H}_{ij}^{(4)},

(B58)

where the part of (
B57
) proportional to
\delta_{ij}
is still supressed in the final perturbation.
However, the traceless definition (
B57
) makes it possible to now directly define the traceless Hermite moment
\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}

as an integral over
\hat{H}_{ij}^{(4)}

\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}=\frac{1}{n_{a}}\int f_{a}\hat{H}_{ij}^{(4)}d^{3}c.

(B59)

This is the main motivation behind irreducible Hermite polynomials, as is further clarified below.

B.3
Irreducible Hermite polynomials

In the work of
Balescu 1988
, the
irreducible
Hermite polynomials are defined through Laguerre-Sonine polynomials, according to
(see equation (G1.4.4), page 326 of Balescu)

\displaystyle H^{(2n)}(\tilde{c})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L_{n}^{1/2}(\frac{\tilde{c}^{2}}{2});

\displaystyle H^{(2n+1)}_{i}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}\tilde{c}_{i}L_{n}^{3/2}(\frac{\tilde{c}^{2}}{2});

\displaystyle H^{(2n)}_{ij}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{15}{8}}(\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\frac{\tilde{c}^{2}}{3}\delta_{ij})L_{n-1}^{5/2}(\frac{\tilde{c}^{2}}{2}).

(B60)

To recover the model
Braginskii 1965
, one only needs (see Table 4.1, page 327 of Balescu)

\displaystyle H^{(3)}_{i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{10}}\tilde{c}_{i}(\tilde{c}^{2}-5);\qquad H^{(5)}_{i}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{70}}\tilde{c}_{i}(\tilde{c}^{4}-14\tilde{c}^{2}+35);

\displaystyle H^{(2)}_{ij}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\big(\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\frac{1}{3}\tilde{c}^{2}\delta_{ij}\big);\qquad H^{(4)}_{ij}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{7}}(\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\frac{1}{3}\tilde{c}^{2}\delta_{ij})(\tilde{c}^{2}-7),

(B61)

yielding Hermite moments

\displaystyle h^{(3)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{5}}\frac{1}{p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}\vec{q}_{i};\qquad h^{(5)}_{i}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{70}p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}\Big[\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}X^{(5)}_{i}-28\vec{q}_{i}\Big];

\displaystyle h^{(2)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}p_{a}}\Pi_{ij}^{(2)};\qquad h^{(4)}_{ij}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{7}p_{a}}\Big[\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}\Pi^{(4)}_{ij}-7\Pi_{ij}^{(2)}\Big].

(B62)

Furthermore, orthogonal relations are

\int\phi^{(0)}H_{i}^{(2n+1)}H_{j}^{(2n+1)}d^{3}c=\delta_{ij};\qquad h_{kl}^{(2n)}\int\phi^{(0)}H_{ij}^{(2n)}H_{kl}^{(2n)}d^{3}c=h_{ij}^{(2n)},

(B63)

yielding perturbation (
B50
), which then recovers perturbation (
B54
), (
B55
) obtained with
reducible polynomials. Both approaches are therefore equivalent, which is further addressed in Section
B.7
.

B.3.1
Higher-order tensorial “anisotropies”

It is useful to clarify what contributions are obtained by using irreducible Hermite polynomials

\displaystyle{H}^{(3)}_{ijk}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})=\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}-\frac{1}{5}\tilde{c}^{2}\big(\delta_{ij}\tilde{c}_{k}+\delta_{jk}\tilde{c}_{i}+\delta_{ik}\tilde{c}_{j}\big);

\displaystyle{H}^{(4)}_{ijkl}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})=\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}\tilde{c}_{l}-\frac{1}{7}\tilde{c}^{2}\big(\delta_{ij}\tilde{c}_{k}\tilde{c}_{l}+\delta_{jk}\tilde{c}_{l}\tilde{c}_{i}+\delta_{kl}\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}+\delta_{li}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}+\delta_{ik}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{l}+\delta_{jl}\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{k}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{35}\tilde{c}^{4}\big(\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk}\big),

(B64)

which
Balescu 1988
calls “anisotropies” (even though they are valid as a perturbation for isotropic Maxwellian).
Importantly, applying trace on (
B64
) yields zero.
The corresponding Hermite moments calculate

\displaystyle h^{(3)}_{ijk}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}\Big[\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{q}}}-\frac{2}{5}\big(\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}\big)^{S}\Big]_{ijk}=\frac{1}{p_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{m_{a}}{T_{a}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{ijk};\qquad h^{(4)}_{ijkl}=\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}^{2}}\sigma^{(4)^{\prime}}_{ijkl},

(B65)

and directly yield the highest-order irreducible parts.

B.4
Decomposition of
X^{(4)}_{ijkl}

We continue with the
reducible
Hermite polynomials.
To decompose the full 4th-order fluid moment
X^{(4)}_{ijkl}
, it is necessary to consider the following perturbation (i.e. the 16-moment model)

\chi_{a}=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{ij}+\frac{1}{28}\hat{h}^{(4)}_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{ij}+\frac{1}{120}\tilde{h}^{(4)}\tilde{H}^{(4)},

(B66)

and by using this perturbation to calculate
X^{(4)}_{ijkl}
. In comparision to the previous perturbation of the 21-moment model, the last term
with Hermite polynomial
\tilde{H}^{(4)}
is new. It is derived with orthogonality relation
\int\phi^{(0)}H^{(4)}H^{(4)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=120
.
We will need the following integrals. Applying contraction

\delta_{r_{3}r_{4}}
at the orthogonality relation (
B10
) yields

\displaystyle\int\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{r_{1}r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{s_{1}}\tilde{c}_{s_{2}}\tilde{c}_{s_{3}}\tilde{c}_{s_{4}}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=\int\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{r_{1}r_{2}}\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}s_{4}}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=

\displaystyle\qquad+2\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{4}}+2\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{4}}+2\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\delta_{r_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{4}}+2\delta_{r_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}+2\delta_{r_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\delta_{r_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{4}}+2\delta_{r_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}+2\delta_{r_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\delta_{r_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}+2\delta_{r_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}+2\delta_{r_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}},

(B67)

and further applying traceless
\hat{h}^{(4)}_{r_{1}r_{2}}
at (
B67
) leads to

\displaystyle\hat{h}^{(4)}_{r_{1}r_{2}}\int\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{r_{1}r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{s_{1}}\tilde{c}_{s_{2}}\tilde{c}_{s_{3}}\tilde{c}_{s_{4}}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=4\Big[\hat{h}_{s_{1}s_{2}}^{(4)}\delta_{s_{3}s_{4}}+\hat{h}_{s_{1}s_{3}}^{(4)}\delta_{s_{2}s_{4}}+\hat{h}_{s_{1}s_{4}}^{(4)}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}+\hat{h}_{s_{2}s_{3}}^{(4)}\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}+\hat{h}_{s_{2}s_{4}}^{(4)}\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}+\hat{h}_{s_{3}s_{4}}^{(4)}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\Big].

(B68)

Applying contraction
\delta_{r_{1}r_{2}}
at (
B67
) and multiplying by
\tilde{h}^{(4)}
yields

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(4)}\int\tilde{H}^{(4)}\tilde{c}_{s_{1}}\tilde{c}_{s_{2}}\tilde{c}_{s_{3}}\tilde{c}_{s_{4}}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=8\tilde{h}^{(4)}\Big[\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{4}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{4}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}\Big].

(B69)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{r_{1}r_{2}}\int\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{r_{1}r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{s_{1}}\tilde{c}_{s_{2}}\tilde{c}_{s_{3}}\tilde{c}_{s_{4}}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=2\Big[\tilde{h}_{s_{1}s_{2}}^{(2)}\delta_{s_{3}s_{4}}+\tilde{h}_{s_{1}s_{3}}^{(2)}\delta_{s_{2}s_{4}}+\tilde{h}_{s_{1}s_{4}}^{(2)}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}+\tilde{h}_{s_{2}s_{3}}^{(2)}\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}+\tilde{h}_{s_{2}s_{4}}^{(2)}\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}+\tilde{h}_{s_{3}s_{4}}^{(2)}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\Big].

(B70)

Results (
B68
), (
B69
), (
B70
) allow us to calcuate the
X^{(4)}_{ijkl}
moment, which becomes

\displaystyle X^{(4)}_{ijkl}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{a}\int f_{a}^{(0)}\Big[1+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{r_{1}r_{2}}\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{r_{1}r_{2}}+\frac{1}{28}\hat{h}^{(4)}_{r_{1}r_{2}}\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{r_{1}r_{2}}+\frac{1}{120}\tilde{h}^{(4)}\tilde{H}^{(4)}\Big]c_{i}c_{j}c_{k}c_{l}d^{3}c

(B71)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{15}\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\Big(15+\tilde{h}^{(4)}\Big)\Big(\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{jk}\delta_{il}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{7}\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\Big[\big(\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}+7\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(2)}\big)\delta_{kl}+\big(\hat{h}_{ik}^{(4)}+7\tilde{h}_{ik}^{(2)}\big)\delta_{jl}+\big(\hat{h}_{il}^{(4)}+7\tilde{h}_{il}^{(2)}\big)\delta_{jk}

\displaystyle\quad+\big(\hat{h}_{jk}^{(4)}+7\tilde{h}_{jk}^{(2)}\big)\delta_{il}+\big(\hat{h}_{jl}^{(4)}+7\tilde{h}_{jl}^{(2)}\big)\delta_{ik}+\big(\hat{h}_{kl}^{(4)}+7\tilde{h}_{kl}^{(2)}\big)\delta_{ij}\Big].

Form (
B71
) nicely shows how various parts of perturbation (
B66
) contribute to the decomposition, including the
new
\tilde{h}^{(4)}
. Prescribing Hermite closures
\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}=0
,
\tilde{h}^{(4)}=0
recovers decomposition (
B30
) used
in the Burgers-Schunk model. Finally, rewritten with fluid moments

\Pi^{(4)}_{ij}=\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\big(\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}+7\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(2)}\big);\qquad\widetilde{X}^{(4)}=\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}\tilde{h}^{(4)};\qquad X^{(4)}=15\frac{p_{a}^{2}}{\rho_{a}}+\widetilde{X}^{(4)},

(B72)

and representing all other terms that were not obtained from (
B66
) by traceless
\sigma^{(4)^{\prime}}_{ijkl}

(which represents the highest-order irreducible part of
X_{ijkl}^{(4)}
), the decomposition becomes

\displaystyle X_{ijkl}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{15}X^{(4)}\big(\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk}\big)

(B73)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{7}\Big[\Pi_{ij}^{(4)}\delta_{kl}+\Pi_{ik}^{(4)}\delta_{jl}+\Pi_{il}^{(4)}\delta_{jk}+\Pi_{jk}^{(4)}\delta_{il}+\Pi_{jl}^{(4)}\delta_{ik}+\Pi_{kl}^{(4)}\delta_{ij}\Big]+\sigma_{ijkl}^{(4)^{\prime}},

or equivalently

\displaystyle X_{ijkl}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{1}{35}X^{(4)}\big(\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk}\big)

(B74)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{7}\Big[X^{(4)}_{ij}\delta_{kl}+X^{(4)}_{ik}\delta_{jl}+X^{(4)}_{il}\delta_{jk}+X^{(4)}_{jk}\delta_{il}+X^{(4)}_{jl}\delta_{ik}+X^{(4)}_{kl}\delta_{ij}\Big]+\sigma_{ijkl}^{(4)^{\prime}}.

Decomposition (
B73
) is equivalent to equation (30.22) of
Grad 1958
. Essentially, any tensorial moment can be decomposed by
subtracting all the possible contractions of that moment. Note that simply prescribing closure
\Pi_{ij}^{(4)}=0
in (
B73
)
would be erroneous, unless one also prescribes
\Pi_{ij}^{(2)}=0
as well. Correct simplification of (
B73
) is
obtained by prescribing Hermite closure
\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}=0
, meaning by prescribing fluid closure
\Pi_{ij}^{(4)}=7(p_{a}/\rho_{a})\Pi_{ij}^{(2)}
.
Additionally, one can also prescribe Hermite closure
\tilde{h}^{(4)}=0
, which is equivalent to fluid closure
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}=0
.

B.5
Decomposition of
X^{(5)}_{ijklm}

We only use simplified perturbation

\chi_{a}=\frac{1}{10}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(3)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(3)}+\frac{1}{280}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(5)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(5)}.

(B75)

By using this perturbation, it is possible to calculate the 5th-order fluid moment

\displaystyle X^{(5)}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}s_{4}s_{5}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{35}\frac{p_{a}^{5/2}}{\rho_{a}^{3/2}}\Big[\big(\tilde{h}_{s_{1}}^{(5)}+14\tilde{h}_{s_{1}}^{(3)}\big)\big(\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{s_{4}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{2}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{2}s_{5}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{4}}\big)

(B76)

\displaystyle+\big(\tilde{h}_{s_{2}}^{(5)}+14\tilde{h}_{s_{2}}^{(3)}\big)\big(\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{s_{4}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{5}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{4}}\big)

\displaystyle+\big(\tilde{h}_{s_{3}}^{(5)}+14\tilde{h}_{s_{3}}^{(3)}\big)\big(\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{s_{4}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{5}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{4}}\big)

\displaystyle+\big(\tilde{h}_{s_{4}}^{(5)}+14\tilde{h}_{s_{4}}^{(3)}\big)\big(\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{5}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}\big)

\displaystyle+\big(\tilde{h}_{s_{5}}^{(5)}+14\tilde{h}_{s_{5}}^{(3)}\big)\big(\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{4}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{4}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}\big)\Big].

Because we considered simplified perturbation (
B75
), we do not consider full decomposition with
\sigma^{(5)}\,{}^{\prime}
.
Prescribing Hermite closure
\tilde{h}^{(5)}_{i}=0
yields previously obtained decomposition (
B37
). Finally, by switching from Hermite to fluid moments

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{i}^{(3)}=2\frac{\rho_{a}^{1/2}}{p_{a}^{3/2}}\vec{q}_{i};\qquad\tilde{h}_{i}^{(5)}=\frac{\rho_{a}^{1/2}}{p_{a}^{3/2}}\Big(\frac{\rho_{a}}{p_{a}}X^{(5)}_{i}-28\vec{q}_{i}\Big);\qquad\tilde{h}_{i}^{(5)}+14\tilde{h}_{i}^{(3)}=\frac{\rho_{a}^{3/2}}{p_{a}^{5/2}}X^{(5)}_{i},

(B77)

the decomposition becomes

\displaystyle X^{(5)}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}s_{4}s_{5}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{35}\Big[X_{s_{1}}^{(5)}\big(\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{s_{4}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{2}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{2}s_{5}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{4}}\big)

(B78)

\displaystyle+X_{s_{2}}^{(5)}\big(\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{s_{4}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{5}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{4}}\big)

\displaystyle+X_{s_{3}}^{(5)}\big(\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{s_{4}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{5}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{4}}\big)

\displaystyle+X_{s_{4}}^{(5)}\big(\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{5}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{5}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}\big)

\displaystyle+X_{s_{5}}^{(5)}\big(\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{s_{3}s_{4}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{4}}+\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}\big)\Big].

As a double-check, applying contraction
\delta_{s_{4}s_{5}}
at the last expression yields

\displaystyle X^{(5)}_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}}=\frac{1}{5}\Big[X_{s_{1}}^{(5)}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}+X_{s_{2}}^{(5)}\delta_{s_{1}s_{3}}+X_{s_{3}}^{(5)}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\Big],

(B79)

and applying another contraction yields an identity. Note that it is not possible to perform closure

\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}=0
, such a closure would be erroneous (unless
\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}=0
is prescribed as well). Instead, one needs to perform closure at the
Hermite moment
\tilde{h}_{i}^{(5)}=0
, or in another words, the correct closure is
\vec{\boldsymbol{X}}^{(5)}=28(p_{a}/\rho_{a})\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}
.

B.6
Table of useful integrals

The Hermite polynomials allows one to built the hierarchy of following integrals.
One introduces weight

\phi^{(0)}=\frac{e^{-\tilde{c}^{2}/2}}{(2\pi)^{3/2}},

(B80)

and for any odd “m” the following integral holds

m=\textrm{odd}:\qquad\int\tilde{c}_{r_{1}}\tilde{c}_{r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{r_{3}}\ldots\tilde{c}_{r_{m}}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=0.

(B81)

The validity of (
B81
) can be shown by using “common-sense” symmetries and Gaussian integration,
or by rewriting the integral with pairs of Hermite polynomials, one of even-order and one of odd-order,

\tilde{H}^{(r_{m}+1)/2}\tilde{H}^{(r_{m}-1)/2}
(where the result of integration is zero), and a hierarchy of lower-order
integrals that will also be odd-even pairs, yielding zero.

A particular case of the orthogonality theorem is, that for any
m\neq 0
, an integral over any single Hermite polynomial with
weight
\phi^{(0)}
is zero

m\neq 0:\qquad\int\tilde{H}^{(m)}_{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}\ldots r_{m}}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=0.

(B82)

The two rules (
B81
), (
B82
) allow one to calculate integrals for any even “m” number of
velocities
\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}
, such as
\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}
, which would be otherwise
very difficult to do just by using “common-sense” symmetries and Gaussian integration. Actually, for “m” being even,
quicker than using (
B82
) is to rewrite the integrals into
\tilde{H}^{(m/2)}\tilde{H}^{(m/2)}
,
and use ortogonality relations (
B9
). A very useful integral also reads

m<n:\qquad\int\tilde{c}_{r_{1}}\ldots\tilde{c}_{r_{m}}H^{(n)}_{s_{1}\ldots s_{n}}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=0,

(B83)

which validity is easily shown by rewriting the
\tilde{c}_{r_{1}}\ldots\tilde{c}_{r_{m}}
with
H^{(m)}_{r_{1}\ldots r_{m}}

(where the result of integration is zero) and a hierarchy of lower-order Hermite polynomials where the result of
integration is also zero.

It is possible to build the following table when “m” is even

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1;

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{ij};

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}\tilde{c}_{l}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{jk}\delta_{il};

(B84)

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{r_{1}}\tilde{c}_{r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{r_{3}}\tilde{c}_{s_{1}}\tilde{c}_{s_{2}}\tilde{c}_{s_{3}}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\big(\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{3}}+\delta_{r_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{1}s_{2}}\big(\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{3}}+\delta_{r_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}}\big)+\delta_{r_{1}s_{3}}\big(\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{2}}+\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{1}r_{2}}\big(\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{3}}+\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}}+\delta_{s_{3}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{1}r_{3}}\big(\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{3}}+\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}+\delta_{s_{3}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{2}r_{3}}\big(\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{1}s_{3}}+\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}+\delta_{s_{3}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{1}s_{2}}\big).

(B85)

These integrals can be used to obtain other useful integrals, for example valid for any (3-dimensional) vector
\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=\delta_{ij}\vec{q}_{k}+\delta_{jk}\vec{q}_{i}+\delta_{ki}\vec{q}_{j}=\big[\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}\big]^{S}_{ijk};

(B86)

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{r_{1}}\tilde{c}_{r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{r_{3}}\tilde{c}_{s_{1}}\tilde{c}_{s_{2}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\big(\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\vec{q}_{r_{3}}+\vec{q}_{r_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{1}s_{2}}\big(\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\vec{q}_{r_{3}}+\vec{q}_{r_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}}\big)+\vec{q}_{r_{1}}\big(\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{2}}+\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{1}r_{2}}\big(\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\vec{q}_{r_{3}}+\vec{q}_{s_{2}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}}+\vec{q}_{s_{1}}\delta_{r_{3}s_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{1}r_{3}}\big(\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\vec{q}_{r_{2}}+\vec{q}_{s_{2}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}+\vec{q}_{s_{1}}\delta_{r_{2}s_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{2}r_{3}}\big(\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\vec{q}_{r_{1}}+\vec{q}_{s_{2}}\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}+\vec{q}_{s_{1}}\delta_{r_{1}s_{2}}\big).

(B87)

and by further contractions

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}\tilde{c}^{2}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=7\big(\delta_{ij}\vec{q}_{k}+\delta_{jk}\vec{q}_{i}+\delta_{ki}\vec{q}_{j}\big)=7\big[\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}\big]^{S}_{ijk};

(B88)

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}\Big(1-\frac{\tilde{c}^{2}}{5}\Big)(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=-\frac{2}{5}\big[\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{I}}}\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}\big]^{S}_{ijk}.

(B89)

As a quick double-check of the above results, by performing further contractions

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}^{2}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=5\vec{q}_{i};\qquad\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}^{4}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\cdot\vec{\boldsymbol{q}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=35\vec{q}_{i},

(B90)

which is easy to verify directly.

Similarly, for a triple contraction with any fully symmetric 3rd-rank tensor
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{q}}}

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}:\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{q}}}\cdot\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=6\big(q_{ijk}+\vec{q}_{i}\delta_{jk}+\vec{q}_{j}\delta_{ik}+\vec{q}_{k}\delta_{ij}\big);

(B91)

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}^{2}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}:\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{q}}}\cdot\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=42\vec{q}_{i},

(B92)

where one defines vector
\vec{\boldsymbol{q}}=(1/2)\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{q}}}
. Finally, for any (
3\times 3
) matrix
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{A}}}

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}:\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{A}}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=A_{ij}+A_{ji}+(\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{A}}})\delta_{ij};

(B93)

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{r_{1}}\tilde{c}_{r_{2}}\tilde{c}_{r_{3}}\tilde{c}_{s_{1}}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}:\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{A}}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\big(A_{r_{2}r_{3}}+A_{r_{3}r_{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\big(A_{r_{1}r_{3}}+A_{r_{3}r_{1}}\big)+\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}}\big(A_{r_{1}r_{2}}+A_{r_{2}r_{1}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{1}r_{2}}\big(A_{r_{3}s_{1}}+A_{s_{1}r_{3}}+(\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{A}}})\delta_{r_{3}s_{1}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{1}r_{3}}\big(A_{r_{2}s_{1}}+A_{s_{1}r_{2}}+(\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{A}}})\delta_{r_{2}s_{1}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\delta_{r_{2}r_{3}}\big(A_{r_{1}s_{1}}+A_{s_{1}r_{1}}+(\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{A}}})\delta_{r_{1}s_{1}}\big),

(B94)

and by further contractions

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}^{2}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}:\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{A}}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=5\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{A}}};

(B95)

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}^{2}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}:\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{A}}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=7\big(A_{ij}+A_{ji}+(\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{A}}})\delta_{ij}\big);

(B96)

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}^{4}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}:\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{A}}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=35\textrm{Tr}\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{A}}},

(B97)

and so for symmetric traceless matrix
\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}}

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}:\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=2\Pi_{ij};

(B98)

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}^{2}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}:\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=14\Pi_{ij};

(B99)

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}\tilde{c}_{l}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{c}}:\bar{\bar{\boldsymbol{\Pi}}})\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle\qquad=2\big(\delta_{ij}\Pi_{kl}+\delta_{ik}\Pi_{jl}+\delta_{il}\Pi_{jk}+\delta_{jk}\Pi_{il}+\delta_{jl}\Pi_{ik}+\delta_{kl}\Pi_{ij}\big).

(B100)

A curious reader might find the following integrals useful

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}\tilde{c}_{l}\tilde{c}^{2}\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=7\Big[\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}+\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk}\Big];

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\tilde{c}_{k}\tilde{c}_{l}(\tilde{c}^{2}-7)\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=0;

\displaystyle\int\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}(\tilde{c}^{2}-5)\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=0;

\displaystyle\int(\tilde{c}^{2}-3)\phi^{(0)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=0.

(B101)

B.7
General n-th order perturbation

The hierarchy of simplified
reducible
Hermite polymomials (with tilde) can be calculated directly from (
B3
) as

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(1)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{i};

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\delta_{ij};

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{i}(\tilde{c}^{2}-5);

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}(\tilde{c}^{2}-7)-\delta_{ij}(\tilde{c}^{2}-5);

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(5)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{i}(\tilde{c}^{4}-14\tilde{c}^{2}+35);

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(6)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}(\tilde{c}^{4}-18\tilde{c}^{2}+63)-\delta_{ij}(\tilde{c}^{4}-14\tilde{c}^{2}+35);

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(7)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{i}(\tilde{c}^{6}-27\tilde{c}^{4}+189\tilde{c}^{2}-315);

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(8)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}(\tilde{c}^{6}-33\tilde{c}^{4}+297\tilde{c}^{2}-693)-\delta_{ij}(\tilde{c}^{6}-27\tilde{c}^{4}+189\tilde{c}^{2}-315);

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(9)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{i}(\tilde{c}^{8}-44\tilde{c}^{6}+594\tilde{c}^{4}-2772\tilde{c}^{2}+3465),

(B102)

and fully contracted ones for the even orders are

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}^{2}-3;

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}^{4}-10\tilde{c}^{2}+15;

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(6)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}^{6}-21\tilde{c}^{4}+105\tilde{c}^{2}-105;

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(8)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}^{8}-36\tilde{c}^{6}+378\tilde{c}^{4}-1260\tilde{c}^{2}+945.

(B103)

The even-order polynomials
\tilde{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}
can be rewritten into

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{c}^{2}\big)+\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{H}^{(2)};

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{c}^{2}\big)(\tilde{c}^{2}-7)+\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{H}^{(4)};

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(6)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{c}^{2}\big)(\tilde{c}^{4}-18\tilde{c}^{2}+63)+\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{H}^{(6)};

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(8)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{c}^{2}\big)(\tilde{c}^{6}-33\tilde{c}^{4}+297\tilde{c}^{2}-693)+\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{H}^{(8)}.

(B104)

The orthogonality relations can be calculated as

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(1)}_{i}\tilde{H}^{(1)}_{j}d^{3}\tilde{c}=\delta_{ij};\qquad\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(2)}\tilde{H}^{(2)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=6;

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{i}\tilde{H}^{(3)}_{j}d^{3}\tilde{c}=10\delta_{ij};\qquad\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(4)}\tilde{H}^{(4)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=120;

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(5)}_{i}\tilde{H}^{(5)}_{j}d^{3}\tilde{c}=280\delta_{ij};\qquad\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(6)}\tilde{H}^{(6)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=5040;

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(7)}_{i}\tilde{H}^{(7)}_{j}d^{3}\tilde{c}=15120\delta_{ij};\qquad\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(8)}\tilde{H}^{(8)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=362880;

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(9)}_{i}\tilde{H}^{(9)}_{j}d^{3}\tilde{c}=1330560\delta_{ij},

together with

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{kl}^{(2)}\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(2)}_{kl}d^{3}\tilde{c}=2\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(2)};\qquad\hat{h}_{kl}^{(4)}\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(4)}_{kl}d^{3}\tilde{c}=28\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)};

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{kl}^{(6)}\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(6)}_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(6)}_{kl}d^{3}\tilde{c}=1008\hat{h}_{ij}^{(6)};\qquad\hat{h}_{kl}^{(8)}\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(8)}_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(8)}_{kl}d^{3}\tilde{c}=66528\hat{h}_{ij}^{(8)},

(B106)

where we used traceless Hermite moments (with hat)

\hat{h}_{ij}^{(2n)}=\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(2n)}-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{ij}\tilde{h}^{(2n)},

(B107)

with
\tilde{h}^{(2)}=0
(so that
\hat{h}^{(2)}_{ij}=\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{ij}
). Perturbation of the distribution function then becomes

\displaystyle\chi_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(2)}\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(2)}+\frac{1}{10}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(3)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(3)}+\frac{1}{28}\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(4)}+\frac{1}{120}\tilde{h}^{(4)}\tilde{H}^{(4)}+\frac{1}{280}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(5)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(5)}

(B108)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{1008}\hat{h}_{ij}^{(6)}\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(6)}+\frac{1}{5040}\tilde{h}^{(6)}\tilde{H}^{(6)}+\frac{1}{15120}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(7)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(7)}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{66528}\hat{h}_{ij}^{(8)}\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(8)}+\frac{1}{362880}\tilde{h}^{(8)}\tilde{H}^{(8)}+\frac{1}{1330560}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(9)}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(9)}+\cdots.

Corresponding perturbation with the irreducible polynomials reads

\displaystyle\chi_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{h}_{ij}^{(2)}{H}_{ij}^{(2)}+{h}_{i}^{(3)}{H}_{i}^{(3)}+{h}_{ij}^{(4)}{H}_{ij}^{(4)}+{h}^{(4)}{H}^{(4)}+{h}_{i}^{(5)}{H}_{i}^{(5)}

(B109)

\displaystyle{h}_{ij}^{(6)}{H}_{ij}^{(6)}+{h}^{(6)}{H}^{(6)}+{h}_{i}^{(7)}{H}_{i}^{(7)}+{h}_{ij}^{(8)}{H}_{ij}^{(8)}+{h}^{(8)}{H}^{(8)}+{h}_{i}^{(9)}{H}_{i}^{(9)}+\cdots,

i.e. no normalization constants are explicitly present. Now one then can clearly see the motivation behind
the definition of irreducible polynomials of
Balescu 1988
, where
direct relation between irreducible (no tilde) and reducible (tilde) Hermite polynomials can be shown to be

\displaystyle H^{(2n)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{2^{n}n!(2n+1)!!}\Big)^{1/2}\tilde{H}^{(2n)};

\displaystyle H^{(2n+1)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{3}{2^{n}n!(2n+3)!!}\Big)^{1/2}\tilde{H}_{i}^{(2n+1)};

\displaystyle H^{(2n)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{15}{2^{n}(n-1)!(2n+3)!!}\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(2n)}-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(2n)}\Big).

(B110)

Up to the normalization constants (which can be viewed as coming from the orthogonality relations), scalar and vector polynomials are equivalent to each other.
The only difference is for matrices
H^{(2n)}_{ij}
, where the irreducible polynomials are defined as traceless. Multiplying (
B110
) by

f_{a}/n_{a}
and integrating over
d^{3}c
yields analogous relations for the Hermite moments

\displaystyle h^{(2n)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{2^{n}n!(2n+1)!!}\Big)^{1/2}\tilde{h}^{(2n)};

\displaystyle h^{(2n+1)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{3}{2^{n}n!(2n+3)!!}\Big)^{1/2}\tilde{h}_{i}^{(2n+1)};

\displaystyle h^{(2n)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{15}{2^{n}(n-1)!(2n+3)!!}\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\underbrace{\tilde{h}_{ij}^{(2n)}-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{ij}\tilde{h}^{(2n)}}_{\hat{h}_{ij}^{(2n)}}\Big).

(B111)

Importantly, because
\hat{h}_{ij}^{(2n)}
is traceless, multiplying (
B110
) and (
B111
) yields

h^{(2n)}_{ij}H^{(2n)}_{ij}=\frac{15}{2^{n}(n-1)!(2n+3)!!}\,\hat{h}_{ij}^{(2n)}\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(2n)}.

(B112)

The two approaches with reducible and irreducible polynomials thus yield the same result, with the only difference being the location of
normalization constants. Furthermore, it feels natural to define traceless polynomials (with hat instead of tilde)

\hat{H}_{ij}^{(2n)}=\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(2n)}-\frac{1}{3}\delta_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(2n)},

(B113)

and on the r.h.s. of (
B112
) replace

\hat{h}^{(2n)}_{ij}\tilde{H}_{ij}^{(2n)}=\hat{h}^{(2n)}_{ij}\hat{H}_{ij}^{(2n)},

(B114)

which holds because
\hat{h}_{ij}^{(2n)}
is traceless. The main advantage of introducing polynomials
\hat{H}_{ij}^{(2n)}
is,
that instead of calculating
\hat{h}^{(2n)}_{ij}
from its definition (
B107
), one can directly define

\hat{h}^{(2n)}_{ij}=\frac{1}{n_{a}}\int f_{a}\hat{H}_{ij}^{(2n)}d^{3}c.

(B115)

Then the two approaches are indeed equivalent because the same polynomials are used,
with the location of normalization constants being an ad-hoc choice.

From the Appendix of
Balescu 1988
, one can guess and then verify the following generalizations for the reducible polynomials

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(2n)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{n}(-1)^{m+n}\frac{n!}{m!(n-m)!}\frac{(2n+1)!!}{(2m+1)!!}\tilde{c}^{2m};

(B116)

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(2n+1)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{i}\sum_{m=0}^{n}(-1)^{m+n}\frac{n!}{m!(n-m)!}\frac{(2n+3)!!}{(2m+3)!!}\tilde{c}^{2m};

(B117)

\displaystyle\tilde{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}\Big(\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{m+n-1}\frac{(n-1)!}{m!(n-m-1)!}\frac{(2n+3)!!}{(2m+5)!!}\tilde{c}^{2m}\Big)-\delta_{ij}\frac{\tilde{H}_{k}^{(2n-1)}}{\tilde{c}_{k}}

(B118)

\displaystyle=\big(\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{c}^{2}\big)\Big(\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{m+n-1}\frac{(n-1)!}{m!(n-m-1)!}\frac{(2n+3)!!}{(2m+5)!!}\tilde{c}^{2m}\Big)+\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{H}^{(2n)};

\displaystyle\hat{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(\tilde{c}_{i}\tilde{c}_{j}-\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}\tilde{c}^{2}\big)\Big(\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{m+n-1}\frac{(n-1)!}{m!(n-m-1)!}\frac{(2n+3)!!}{(2m+5)!!}\tilde{c}^{2m}\Big).

(B119)

Applying trace at (
B118
) yields (
B116
). Similarly, the orthogonal relations are

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(2n)}\tilde{H}^{(2m)}d^{3}\tilde{c}=2^{n}n!(2n+1)!!\,\delta_{nm};

(B120)

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(2n+1)}_{i}\tilde{H}^{(2m+1)}_{j}d^{3}\tilde{c}=\frac{2^{n}n!(2n+3)!!}{3}\,\delta_{ij}\delta_{nm};

(B121)

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(2m)}_{kl}d^{3}\tilde{c}=\frac{2^{n-1}(n-1)!(2n+1)!!}{15}\Big[(2n+3)\big(\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk}\big)+2(n-1)\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}\Big]\delta_{nm};

(B122)

\displaystyle\int\phi^{(0)}\hat{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}\hat{H}^{(2m)}_{kl}d^{3}\tilde{c}=\frac{2^{n-1}(n-1)!(2n+3)!!}{15}\Big[\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk}-\frac{2}{3}\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}\Big]\delta_{nm},

(B123)

and applying
\delta_{ij}\delta_{kl}
on (
B122
) recovers (
B120
). Note that the orders of Hermite moments
“m” and “n” are 1-dimensional and
\delta_{nn}=1
. In contrast, for the indices
\delta_{ii}=3
applies.
Also note that
n!=n!!(n-1)!!
and
2^{n}n!=(2n)!!
, implying
2^{n}n!(2n+1)!!=(2n+1)!
.
Applying traceless

\hat{h}_{kl}^{(2n)}
on (
B122
) or (
B123
) yields orthogonal relation

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{kl}^{(2n)}\int\phi^{(0)}\hat{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}\hat{H}^{(2n)}_{kl}d^{3}\tilde{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{kl}^{(2n)}\int\phi^{(0)}\tilde{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(2n)}_{kl}d^{3}\tilde{c}

(B124)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{n}(n-1)!(2n+3)!!}{15}\hat{h}^{(2n)}_{ij}.

Finally, the general perturbation then can be written as

\displaystyle\chi_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\Big[\frac{15}{2^{n}(n-1)!(2n+3)!!}\hat{h}^{(2n)}_{ij}\hat{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}+\frac{1}{2^{n}n!(2n+1)!!}\tilde{h}^{(2n)}\tilde{H}^{(2n)}

(B125)

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2^{n}n!(2n+3)!!}\tilde{h}^{(2n+1)}_{i}\tilde{H}^{(2n+1)}_{i}\Big],

where for the first term
\tilde{h}^{(2)}=0
(and so
\hat{h}^{(2)}_{ij}=\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{ij}
). Alternativelly,

\hat{h}^{(2n)}_{ij}\hat{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}=\hat{h}^{(2n)}_{ij}\tilde{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}
. Perturbation
(
B125
) is equivalent to perturbation with irreducible polynomials

\displaystyle\chi_{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\Big[{h}^{(2n)}_{ij}{H}^{(2n)}_{ij}+{h}^{(2n)}{H}^{(2n)}+{h}^{(2n+1)}_{i}{H}^{(2n+1)}_{i}\Big],

(B126)

where again
h^{(2)}=0
.

B.8
Hierarchy of MHD Hermite closures

Let us use the 3rd-order moment
X^{(3)}_{i}=2q_{i}
instead of the heat flux, so that no additional factors are present (also note that
X^{(2)}=3p
).
The even-order moments are decomposed according to

X_{ij}^{(2n)}=\frac{\delta_{ij}}{3}X^{(2n)}+\Pi_{ij}^{(2n)},

(B127)

where the scalar part
X^{(2n)}
is further decomposed into its Maxwellian “core” and perturbation
\widetilde{X}^{(2n)}
(with wide tilde) as

X^{(2n)}=(2n+1)!!\frac{p^{n}}{\rho^{n-1}}+\widetilde{X}^{(2n)},

(B128)

so for example

X^{(4)}=15\frac{p^{2}}{\rho}+\widetilde{X}^{(4)};\qquad X^{(6)}=105\frac{p^{3}}{\rho^{2}}+\widetilde{X}^{(6)};\qquad X^{(8)}=945\frac{p^{4}}{\rho^{3}}+\widetilde{X}^{(8)}.

(B129)

Then by using Hermite polynomials (
B102
)-(
B104
), one
calculates hierarchy of Hermite moments

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(3)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\rho^{1/2}}{p^{3/2}}X^{(3)}_{i};\qquad\tilde{h}^{(4)}=\frac{\rho}{p}\widetilde{X}^{(4)};

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(5)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\rho^{1/2}}{p^{3/2}}\Big[\frac{\rho}{p}X^{(5)}_{i}-14X^{(3)}_{i}\Big];\qquad\tilde{h}^{(6)}=\frac{\rho}{p^{2}}\Big[\frac{\rho}{p}\widetilde{X}^{(6)}-21\widetilde{X}^{(4)}\Big];

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(7)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\rho^{1/2}}{p^{3/2}}\Big[\frac{\rho^{2}}{p^{2}}X^{(7)}_{i}-27\frac{\rho}{p}X^{(5)}_{i}+189X_{i}^{(3)}\Big];

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(8)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\rho}{p^{2}}\Big[\frac{\rho^{2}}{p^{2}}\widetilde{X}^{(8)}-36\frac{\rho}{p}\widetilde{X}^{(6)}+378\widetilde{X}^{(4)}\Big];

\displaystyle\tilde{h}^{(9)}_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\rho^{1/2}}{p^{3/2}}\Big[\frac{\rho^{3}}{p^{3}}X^{(9)}_{i}-44\frac{\rho^{2}}{p^{2}}X^{(7)}_{i}+594\frac{\rho}{p}X^{(5)}_{i}-2772X^{(3)}_{i}\Big],

(B130)

together with

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{ij}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\Big[\frac{\rho}{p}\Pi_{ij}^{(4)}-7\Pi_{ij}^{(2)}\Big];

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{ij}^{(6)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\Big[\frac{\rho^{2}}{p^{2}}\Pi_{ij}^{(6)}-18\frac{\rho}{p}\Pi_{ij}^{(4)}+63\Pi_{ij}^{(2)}\Big];

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{ij}^{(8)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\Big[\frac{\rho^{3}}{p^{3}}\Pi_{ij}^{(8)}-33\frac{\rho^{2}}{p^{2}}\Pi_{ij}^{(6)}+297\frac{\rho}{p}\Pi_{ij}^{(4)}-693\Pi_{ij}^{(2)}\Big].

(B131)

Prescribing the last retained Hermite moment to be zero, then yields corresponding fluid closures which are summarized
in Section
VIII.6
, Tables
VIII.3
&
VIII.4
.

Propagation along the B-field (ion-acoustic mode)

For a propagation parallel to the mean magnetic field which is applied in the z-direction, linearized equations without collisions read

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\rho}{\partial t}+\rho_{0}\partial_{z}u_{z}=0;\qquad\quad\frac{\partial u_{z}}{\partial t}+\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\partial_{z}p=0;

\displaystyle\frac{\partial p}{\partial t}+\frac{5}{3}p_{0}\partial_{z}u_{z}+\frac{2}{3}\partial_{z}q_{z}=0;

\displaystyle\frac{\partial q_{z}}{\partial t}+\frac{1}{6}\partial_{z}X^{(4)}-\frac{5}{2}\frac{p_{0}}{\rho_{0}}\partial_{z}p=0;

\displaystyle\frac{\partial X^{(4)}}{\partial t}+\partial_{z}X^{(5)}_{z}+\frac{7}{3}X^{(4)}_{0}\partial_{z}u_{z}=0;

\displaystyle\frac{\partial X^{(5)}_{z}}{\partial t}+\frac{1}{3}\partial_{z}X^{(6)}-\frac{7}{3}\frac{X^{(4)}_{0}}{\rho_{0}}\partial_{z}p=0,

(B132)

where all the variables are scalars. We are neglecting collisions and viscosities, to make direct comparison with the CGL
model in the next section. The even-order moments are decomposed into a Maxwellian “core” and tilde
perturbations with (
B129
), so their mean values are
X^{(4)}_{0}=15p_{0}^{2}/\rho_{0}
and
X^{(6)}_{0}=105p_{0}^{3}/\rho_{0}^{2}
.
These moments are thus linearized according to

X^{(4)}\overset{\textrm{lin.}}{=}X^{(4)}_{0}\Big(2\frac{p}{p_{0}}-\frac{\rho}{\rho_{0}}\Big)+\widetilde{X}^{(4)};\qquad X^{(6)}\overset{\textrm{lin.}}{=}X^{(6)}_{0}\Big(3\frac{p}{p_{0}}-2\frac{\rho}{\rho_{0}}\Big)+\widetilde{X}^{(6)},

(B133)

and the last three equations of (
B132
) then become

\displaystyle\frac{\partial q_{z}}{\partial t}+\frac{1}{6}\partial_{z}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}+\frac{5}{2}\frac{p_{0}}{\rho_{0}}\big(\partial_{z}p-\frac{p_{0}}{\rho_{0}}\partial_{z}\rho\big)=0;

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\widetilde{X}^{(4)}}{\partial t}+\partial_{z}X^{(5)}_{z}-20\frac{p_{0}}{\rho_{0}}\partial_{z}q_{z}=0;

\displaystyle\frac{\partial X^{(5)}_{z}}{\partial t}+\frac{1}{3}\partial_{z}\widetilde{X}^{(6)}+70\frac{p_{0}^{2}}{\rho_{0}^{2}}\big(\partial_{z}p-\frac{p_{0}}{\rho_{0}}\partial_{z}\rho\big)=0.

(B134)

Prescribing closure at the last retained moment, yields dispersion relations in the variable

\zeta=\omega/(|k_{\parallel}|v_{th})
that are summarized in Table
B.1
.

Closure

Dispersion relation

Solution
\pm\zeta=

\tilde{h}^{(3)}_{z}=0
;

\zeta^{2}-5/6=0
;

0.913
;

\tilde{h}^{(4)}=0
;

\zeta^{4}-(5/3)\zeta^{2}+(5/12)=0
;

0.553;\quad 1.166
;

\tilde{h}^{(5)}_{z}=0
;

\zeta^{4}-(7/3)\zeta^{2}+(35/36)=0
;

0.737;\quad 1.338
;

\tilde{h}^{(6)}=0
;

\zeta^{6}-(7/2)\zeta^{4}+(35/12)\zeta^{2}-(35/72)=0
;

0.471;\quad 0.966;\quad 1.531
;

{X}^{(5)}_{z}=0
;

\zeta^{4}-(35/36)=0
;

0.99;\quad 0.99\,i
;

\widetilde{X}^{(6)}=0
;

\zeta^{6}-(35/12)\zeta^{2}+(35/36)=0
;

0.59;\quad 1.23;\quad 1.36\,i
;

Table B.1:
Summary of Hermite closures and corresponding dispersion relations for parallel propagating ion-acoustic mode (electrons are cold),
where
\zeta=\omega/(|k_{\parallel}|v_{\textrm{th}})
. With Hermite closures (upper half of the table), no spurious instabilities are present.
Unphysical instabilities appear if one prescribes
erroneous fluid closures at the last retained moment
X^{(5)}_{z}=0
or
\widetilde{X}^{(6)}=0
(lower half of the table). However, if one prescribes
at the same time
\widetilde{X}^{(6)}=0
and
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}=0
, the system is again well-defined with dispersion relation equivalent
to closure
\tilde{h}^{(4)}=0
.

The example clearly demonstrates that Landau fluid closures are actually
not
required to
go beyond the 4th-order moment, which contradicts a claim in the last paragraph of
Hunana et al. 2018
, and also
in various parts of
Hunana et al. 2019a
;
Hunana et al. 2019b
(see e.g. Section 12.2 in Part 1). Obviously, closures
X^{(5)}_{z}=0
or
\widetilde{X}^{(6)}=0
are not allowed
by the fluid hierarchy (unless
q_{z}=0
or
\widetilde{X}^{(4)}=0
as well). Instead, for moments of order
n\geq 5
,
one needs to construct “classical” closures at the Hermite moments. Nevertheless, all the Landau fluid closures reported
in the above papers are constructed correctly.

Out of curiosity, prescribing closures with a free parameter “
a
” as
X^{(5)}_{z}=28a(p_{0}/\rho_{0})q_{z}
or

\widetilde{X}^{(6)}=21a(p_{0}/\rho_{0})\widetilde{X}^{(4)}
yields the following dispersion relations

\displaystyle\zeta^{4}-\frac{7a}{3}\zeta^{2}+\frac{35a}{18}-\frac{35}{36}=0;

(B135)

\displaystyle\zeta^{6}-\frac{7a}{2}\zeta^{4}+\Big(\frac{35a}{6}-\frac{35}{12}\Big)\zeta^{2}-\frac{35a}{24}+\frac{35}{36}=0.

(B136)

The
X^{(5)}_{z}
closure with dispersion relation (
B135
) yields an instability for
a<1/2
, and the
\widetilde{X}^{(6)}
closure with
(
B136
) yields an instability for
a<2/3
. There is therefore a lot of closures which do not create these unphysical
instabilities.

Finally, the situation is saved by completely de-coupling the odd and even moments, for example prescribing

\widetilde{X}^{(6)}=\widetilde{X}^{(4)}=0
, so that equations (
B134
) are replaced by

\displaystyle\frac{\partial q_{z}}{\partial t}+\frac{5}{2}\frac{p_{0}}{\rho_{0}}\big(\partial_{z}p-\frac{p_{0}}{\rho_{0}}\partial_{z}\rho\big)=0;

\displaystyle\frac{\partial X^{(5)}_{z}}{\partial t}+70\frac{p_{0}^{2}}{\rho_{0}^{2}}\big(\partial_{z}p-\frac{p_{0}}{\rho_{0}}\partial_{z}\rho\big)=0.

(B137)

Dispersion relation of this model is equivalent to closure
\tilde{h}^{(4)}=0
.

B.9
Hierarchy of CGL (parallel) Hermite closures

The hierarchy of 1D Hermite polynomials calculates (with weight
\exp(-\tilde{c}^{2}/2)
)

\displaystyle{H}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c};

\displaystyle{H}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}^{2}-1;

\displaystyle{H}^{(3)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}(\tilde{c}^{2}-3);

\displaystyle{H}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}^{4}-6\tilde{c}^{2}+3;

\displaystyle{H}^{(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}(\tilde{c}^{4}-10\tilde{c}^{2}+15);

\displaystyle{H}^{(6)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}^{6}-15\tilde{c}^{4}+45\tilde{c}^{2}-15;

\displaystyle{H}^{(7)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}(\tilde{c}^{6}-21\tilde{c}^{4}+105\tilde{c}^{2}-105);

\displaystyle{H}^{(8)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}^{8}-28\tilde{c}^{6}+210\tilde{c}^{4}-420\tilde{c}^{2}+105;

\displaystyle{H}^{(9)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{c}(\tilde{c}^{8}-36\tilde{c}^{6}+378\tilde{c}^{4}-1260\tilde{c}^{2}+945),

(B138)

further yielding the following hierarchy of Hermite moments

\displaystyle h^{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0;\qquad h^{(2)}=0;

\displaystyle h^{(3)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\rho^{1/2}}{p^{3/2}}X^{(3)};\qquad h^{(4)}=\frac{\rho}{p^{2}}\widetilde{X}^{(4)};

\displaystyle h^{(5)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\rho^{1/2}}{p^{3/2}}\Big(\frac{\rho}{p}X^{(5)}-10X^{(3)}\Big);\qquad h^{(6)}=\frac{\rho}{p^{2}}\Big(\frac{\rho}{p}\widetilde{X}^{(6)}-15\widetilde{X}^{(4)}\Big);

\displaystyle h^{(7)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\rho^{1/2}}{p^{3/2}}\Big(\frac{\rho^{2}}{p^{2}}X^{(7)}-21\frac{\rho}{p}X^{(5)}+105X^{(3)}\Big);

\displaystyle h^{(8)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\rho}{p^{2}}\Big(\frac{\rho^{2}}{p^{2}}\widetilde{X}^{(8)}-28\frac{\rho}{p}\widetilde{X}^{(6)}+210\widetilde{X}^{(4)}\Big);

\displaystyle h^{(9)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\rho^{1/2}}{p^{3/2}}\Big(\frac{\rho^{3}}{p^{3}}X^{(9)}-36\frac{\rho^{2}}{p^{2}}X^{(7)}+378\frac{\rho}{p}X^{(5)}-1260X^{(3)}\Big),

(B139)

where the even moments were separated into

\displaystyle X^{(4)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3\frac{p^{2}}{\rho}+\widetilde{X}^{(4)};\qquad X^{(6)}=15\frac{p^{3}}{\rho^{2}}+\widetilde{X}^{(6)};\qquad X^{(8)}=105\frac{p^{4}}{\rho^{3}}+\widetilde{X}^{(8)};

\displaystyle X^{(2n)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2n-1)!!\frac{p^{n}}{\rho^{n-1}}+\widetilde{X}^{(2n)}.

(B140)

This yields the hierarchy of Hermite closures summarized in Table
B.2
.
Note the difference of (
B140
) with the isotropic (MHD) decomposition (
B128
)
(in the 3D CGL geometry one typically uses notation
X^{(4)}=r_{\parallel\parallel}
).

Hermite closures

Fluid closures

{h}^{(3)}=0
;

X^{(3)}=0
;

{h}^{(4)}=0
;

\widetilde{X}^{(4)}=0
;

{h}^{(5)}=0
;

X^{(5)}=10\frac{p}{\rho}X^{(3)}
;

{h}^{(6)}=0
;

\widetilde{X}^{(6)}=15\frac{p}{\rho}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}
;

{h}^{(7)}=0
;

X^{(7)}=21\frac{p}{\rho}X^{(5)}-105\frac{p^{2}}{\rho^{2}}X^{(3)}
;

{h}^{(8)}=0
;

\widetilde{X}^{(8)}=28\frac{p}{\rho}\widetilde{X}^{(6)}-210\frac{p^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\widetilde{X}^{(4)}
;

{h}^{(9)}=0
;

X^{(9)}=36\frac{p}{\rho}X^{(7)}-378\frac{p^{2}}{\rho^{2}}X^{(5)}+1260\frac{p^{3}}{\rho^{3}}X^{(3)}
.

Table B.2:
Summary of Hermite closures for parallel CGL moments, together with corresponding fluid closures.
The usual parallel heat flux
q_{\parallel}=X^{(3)}
. Note that beyond the 4th-order moment both classes start to differ. A general form
corresponding to
h^{(2n+1)}=0
and
h^{(2n)}=0
is given by (
B142
).

Hermite polynomials (
B138
) can be written in a general form

\displaystyle H^{(2n+1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{n}(-1)^{n-m}\frac{(2n+1)!}{2^{n-m}(2m+1)!(n-m)!}\tilde{c}^{2m+1};

\displaystyle H^{(2n)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{n}(-1)^{n-m}\frac{(2n)!}{2^{n-m}(2m)!(n-m)!}\tilde{c}^{2m}.

(B141)

Then it can be shown that prescribing Hermite closure
h^{(2n+1)}=0
or
h^{(2n)}=0
, is equivalent to prescribing fluid closure

\displaystyle X^{(2n+1)}

\displaystyle=
